### CheckInScan

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/CheckIns/CheckInScan.razor.cs:21` · Level 10 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the organizer check-in surface (`@page "/check-in"`, `[Authorize(Roles = "Organizer")]`, `CheckInScan.razor:1-2`). On a head with a camera it loops the shared barcode scanner and posts each badge payload; everywhere else the manual search below the scan area is the whole page. Both paths write through the same endpoints and report the attendee by name.
- **Depends on**: [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice), [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice), [`ISessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#isessionlookupservice), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#iliveeventuiservice), [`IBarcodeScannerService`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#ibarcodescannerservice) and [`IToastService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itoastservice) (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:26-31`); the shared contracts [`BadgePayload`](#badgepayload), [`CheckInScope`](#checkinscope), [`CheckInAttendeeRequest`](#checkinattendeerequest), [`ManualCheckInRequest`](#manualcheckinrequest), [`CheckInResultDTO`](#checkinresultdto); [`LiveEventContext`](group-22-engagement-module.md#liveeventcontext) and [`SessionInfo`](group-22-engagement-module.md#sessioninfo); [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and [`ErrorType`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortype) (`:4`); and its own nested [`ScanOutcome`](#scanoutcome) / [`ScanOutcomeKind`](#scanoutcomekind) plus the [`AttendeeSearchPanel`](#attendeesearchpanel) and [`OrganizerEventPicker`](#organizereventpicker) components.
- **Concept introduced, the capability-gated scan loop.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` and the device-capability abstraction of [ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) meet here. `ScannerAvailable => Scanner.IsSupported` (`:46`, with the ADR named in the comment at `:45`) is the whole capability switch: the markup renders the scan card only when it is true (`CheckInScan.razor:78`), and every head that cannot open a camera falls through to the manual panel with no conditional compilation and no per-platform page. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: scanned text is untrusted input, so `BadgePayload.TryExtractCredential` screens it before any request goes out (`:219`), and a poster or sponsor QR is reported as a skipped scan rather than an error (`:215-218`). `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]`: `CanCheckIn` (`:49-50`) refuses to start a scan or a manual write until a target exists, and the markup renders an inline "pick a target" alert when it is false (`CheckInScan.razor:69-74`).
- **Walkthrough** (teaching order)
  - Constants and state: `MaxOutcomes = 10` bounds the on-screen feed (`:24`); the `CancellationTokenSource` (`:33`), `LifetimeToken` (`:38`), `IsLoading` (`:43`), `_loadError`, `_liveEvent`, the `_events` list of published events that feeds the [`OrganizerEventPicker`](#organizereventpicker) (`:54`), `_sessions` and `_selectedSessionId` (`:52-56`), the `_loadGeneration` counter (`:63`, explained below), the scope field (`:68`), the `_isScanning` flag and the `_outcomes` list (`:70-71`).
  - `LifetimeToken => _cts.LifetimeToken()` (`:38`) is the token every await on the page uses (catalog load, `Scanner.ScanAsync`, both writes, the name lookup). The comment above it (`:35-37`) explains that reading `Token` off a disposed source throws `ObjectDisposedException`; because `Dispose` cancels before it disposes, a handler resuming after navigation gets a cancelled token and stops through its `OperationCanceledException` path.
  - Default scope: `_scope = CheckInScope.Session` with a comment that is real operational knowledge (`:65-68`, repeated for the reader in the markup at `CheckInScan.razor:43-45`), namely that the conference runs door and arrival check-in through TicketLeap, so session check-in is the working path and `Event` scope is kept for future info-desk use.
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:73`) snapshots `_loadGeneration` (`:81`), resolves the current live event (`:84`) and returns early when there is none (`:85-88`). It then reads the published events for the picker through `LiveEventService.GetPublishedEventsAsync` (`:92`), falling back to the current event alone when the list is empty (`:93`), so a failed read degrades to "no picker" rather than an error (comment `:90-91`), and calls `LoadSessionsAsync(_liveEvent.EventId)` (`:95`). The same swallow-cancel shape as the other pages (`:97-100`); the `finally` clears `IsLoading` only when the generation is unchanged (`:101-107`), so a superseded initial load cannot hide the spinner of a newer selection.
  - Event switching: `OnEventSelectedAsync` (`:114`) is the handler the picker's `OnEventSelected` callback targets (`CheckInScan.razor:15`). It ignores an id not in `_events` (`:116-120`), calls `StopScanning()` and sets `_liveEvent` to the chosen event (`:122-123`), and returns `ReloadSessionsAsync(eventId)` (`:124`).
  - `ReloadSessionsAsync` (`:132-158`) is shared by the picker and by `RetryLoadAsync` (`:128-129`), the `OnRetry` target of the failed-load body (`CheckInScan.razor:23-25`), which reloads the selected event's sessions. It bumps `_loadGeneration` first, so anything in flight now belongs to an older generation (comment `:134`, bump `:135-136`), clears the session choice and session list (sessions belong to one event), clears `_loadError` and sets `IsLoading` (`:138-141`), then awaits `LoadSessionsAsync` under the same swallow-cancel and generation-gated `finally` as the initial load (`:143-157`).
  - `LoadSessionsAsync` (`:161-181`) is the shared loader. It snapshots the generation, awaits the `Result`-returning catalog lookup (`:165`), and drops the result if the generation moved while it was in flight (`:166-169`), so a slow earlier load never overwrites a newer selection's list. A failed result sets the load error (`:171-175`); otherwise it filters sessions to the given event id, ordering by start time with `?? DateTime.MaxValue` so unscheduled sessions sort last, then by title under `StringComparer.CurrentCulture` (`:177-180`).
  - Target selection: `OnScopeChangedAsync` stops any running scan when the scope changes (`:183-188`), which prevents a scan started against one target from continuing under another; `OnSessionChanged` just records the selection (`:190`).
  - `StartScanningAsync` (`:197-238`) is the loop. It refuses to start twice or without a target (`:199-202`), then repeatedly awaits `Scanner.ScanAsync(LifetimeToken)` while `_isScanning` holds and cancellation has not been requested (`:207-209`). A `null` payload means cancelled or permission denied, and the documented behaviour (`:192-196`) is that the loop ends quietly, never with an error dialog (`:210-213`). A non-badge payload adds a `NotABadge` outcome, calls `StateHasChanged()` immediately, and continues (`:219-224`): the repaint is deliberate, because the next `ScanAsync` waits on the camera and the operator must see why that code was skipped in the meantime (comment `:215-218`). Otherwise it submits and calls `StateHasChanged()` so the feed paints between scans (`:226-227`).
  - `SubmitScanAsync` (`:242-256`) builds a [`CheckInAttendeeRequest`](#checkinattendeerequest) carrying the credential, the scope, the event id and, only in session scope, the selected session (`:244-250`). It does not catch anything: the write (`CheckInByCredentialAsync(request, LifetimeToken)`, `:254`) returns a `Result` that `RecordResultAsync` turns into a `Failed` row while the loop keeps scanning, and a genuine cancellation propagates to the loop's own handler (`:252-255`).
  - `ManualCheckInAsync` (`:259-297`) is the fallback the panel raises. It toasts a warning when no target is selected (`:261-265`), builds a [`ManualCheckInRequest`](#manualcheckinrequest) by user id (`:269-275`), and on a failed result adds a named `Failed` outcome (`:280`) plus a toast whose wording depends on *why* it failed: a refusal the API stated is shown verbatim through `result.LocalizedErrorMessage(L)`, while an `ErrorType.Unexpected` fault keeps the page's own wording instead of raw diagnostic text (`:282-287`). `[Rubric §11, Security]` and `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: that branch is the boundary between what an organizer may read and what belongs in the logs.
  - `RecordResultAsync` (`:303-320`) turns one response into an outcome. A failed `Result` becomes a `Failed` row (`:305-309`). The scan path has no name to start from because a badge carries only a credential, so it resolves one via `AttendeeLookup.GetDisplayNameAsync(checkIn.UserId, LifetimeToken)` (`:311-315`); the manual path already has one and skips the lookup. Finally the response's `AlreadyCheckedIn` flag selects the outcome kind (`:319`), which is how a repeat scan reads as a recognisable warning instead of an error.
  - Presentation helpers `OutcomeText` / `SeverityFor` / `IconFor` (`:331-356`) and the bounded `AddOutcome` (`:322-329`), covered under [`ScanOutcomeKind`](#scanoutcomekind).
  - `Dispose(bool)` (`:360-375`) clears `_isScanning` **before** cancelling and disposing the token source (`:369-371`), so the loop's condition fails on the next iteration as well as its await being cancelled.
- **Why it's built this way**: one page serving both a camera head and a keyboard head is a deliberate choice, and it is only affordable because the scan capability is behind an injected interface. Treating a duplicate as a first-class outcome rather than an error is the other design decision that matters at a real door: the organizer needs to see who the badge belongs to either way, which is why [`CheckInResultDTO`](#checkinresultdto) carries `AlreadyCheckedIn` instead of returning a conflict (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Shared/CheckIns/CheckInResultDTO.cs:3-7`, `:14`).
- **Where it's used**: routed at `/check-in`, Organizer-only, and surfaced in the nav by [`EngagementUIModule`](#engagementuimodule) with the same role requirement (`EngagementUIModule.cs:28`). It writes through [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice) to the [`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller) endpoints.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: what the camera actually does on each platform is behind [`IBarcodeScannerService`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#ibarcodescannerservice) and is covered in the device-capability chapter, not here.

### OrganizerAttendance

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/CheckIns/OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:18` · Level 10 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the organizer attendance rollup for the current event (`@page "/organizer/attendance"`, `[Authorize(Roles = "Organizer")]`, `OrganizerAttendance.razor:1-2`): one headline arrival figure plus one row per session that has at least one check-in.
- **Depends on**: [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#iliveeventuiservice), [`ISessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#isessionlookupservice) (`OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:20-22`), [`AttendanceStatsDTO`](#attendancestatsdto) and [`SessionAttendanceDTO`](#sessionattendancedto), and its own nested [`SessionAttendanceRow`](#sessionattendancerow). Externals: MudBlazor `MudSimpleTable`/`BreadcrumbItem`, `System.Globalization` in the markup (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:3`), the shared `DetailLoadStateContent` failed-load body, and an injected `IStringLocalizer<OrganizerAttendance>` (`:7`).
- **Concept, client-side enrichment across a service boundary.** `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` and `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` both apply. The class summary states the situation (`OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:11-17`): the stats endpoint reports session identifiers only, because Engagement owns check-ins and Conference owns the session catalog, so titles have to be joined somewhere. This page does it in the client, and `BuildSessionRowsAsync` documents the choice (`:171-175`): the whole catalog is fetched once rather than one request per row, because a busy conference day reports dozens of sessions at a time. That is the N+1 avoidance rule applied to a UI join.
- **Walkthrough**
  - State: the token source (`:24`), the `LifetimeToken` property (`:29`, the disposed-source guard explained in the comment above it), breadcrumbs (`:31`), `IsLoading` (`:34`), the error string, event name, the raw `_stats` and the projected `_rows` (`:36-39`), then the picker state: `_events` (`:40`, the published events) and `_selectedEventId` (`:41`), plus `_loadGeneration` (`:48`), a counter bumped by `OnEventSelectedAsync` to supersede every stats load already in flight (doc comment `:43-47`).
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:50`) snapshots `_loadGeneration` (`:58`), resolves the live event through `GetCurrentEventAsync(LifetimeToken)` (`:61`) and returns early if there is none (`:62-65`), stores its id and name as the selection (`:67-68`), reads the published events for the [`OrganizerEventPicker`](#organizereventpicker) via `GetPublishedEventsAsync` (`:72`) with a fallback to the current event alone when the list is empty (`:73`; comment `:70-71`), and calls `LoadStatsAsync(liveEvent.EventId)` (`:75`). Same swallow-cancel discipline as its siblings (`:77-80`), but the `finally` clears `IsLoading` only when the generation is unchanged (`:81-87`).
  - `OnEventSelectedAsync` (`:91`) is the picker callback target (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:15`). It ignores an id not in `_events` (`:93-97`), swaps in the selected id and name (`:99-100`), and returns `ReloadStatsAsync(eventId)` (`:101`).
  - `ReloadStatsAsync` (`:109-135`) is shared by the picker and by `RetryLoadAsync` (`:105-106`), the `OnRetry` target of the failed-load body (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:23-25`), which reloads the selected event's figures. It bumps `_loadGeneration` first, so anything in flight now belongs to an older generation (`:111-113`), clears `_stats`, `_rows` and the error and sets `IsLoading` (`:115-118`), then awaits `LoadStatsAsync` under the same swallow-cancel and generation-gated `finally` as the initial load (`:120-134`).
  - `LoadStatsAsync` (`:138-169`) is the shared loader: it fetches the rollup with `GetAttendanceStatsAsync(eventId, LifetimeToken)` (`:142`), drops the result if a newer selection superseded it (`:143-146`), sets the localized load error and returns on a failed `Result` (`:148-152`), builds the rows (`:154`), re-checks the generation (`:155-158`), stores `_stats` (`:160`), and if the row build returned `null` clears the rows and raises the load error (`:161-166`), otherwise assigns `_rows` (`:168`). Note the three distinct render states the markup then keys off: the failed-load body with its Retry (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:21-26`), no live event with `_stats` still null (`:27-32`), and the rollup itself (`:33-78`).
  - `BuildSessionRowsAsync` (`:176`) returns `Task<List<SessionAttendanceRow>?>` instead of assigning state itself: it short-circuits to an empty list when the rollup has no session rows, avoiding a pointless catalog fetch (`:178-181`). The catalog fetch itself uses `SessionLookup.GetAllAsync(LifetimeToken)` (`:183`). A failed catalog fetch returns `null` (`:184-187`), which `LoadStatsAsync` turns into cleared rows plus the same load error rather than silently rendering untitled rows. Otherwise it projects each DTO into a [`SessionAttendanceRow`](#sessionattendancerow), substituting a localized `Sessions.UnknownTitle` when the catalog does not know the session (`:191-193`), which keeps a deleted or cross-event session from rendering a blank row. Sorting is `OrderByDescending(Count)` then `ThenBy(Title, StringComparer.CurrentCulture)` (`:195-196`), a culture-aware tie-break, so the busiest sessions lead.
  - `Dispose` (`:201-221`) is the same disposable pattern as the other check-in pages.
- **Why it's built this way**: sorting and labelling once, on load, keeps the markup a pure loop over `_rows` (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:68-74`) with no I/O in the render path. `[Rubric §21, Accessibility]`: that markup is a real `MudSimpleTable` with a `<caption>`, `<th scope="col">` headers and a `<th scope="row">` per session (`OrganizerAttendance.razor:59-76`), not a styled `div` grid, so a screen reader announces the counts with their session names. `[Rubric §27, i18n]`: both the headline figure and each row count are formatted `ToString("N0", CultureInfo.CurrentCulture)` (`:44`, `:72`).
- **Where it's used**: routed at `/organizer/attendance` and surfaced as an Organizer-only nav item by [`EngagementUIModule`](#engagementuimodule) (`EngagementUIModule.cs:29`). Its numbers come from the same check-in rows that [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) writes.

### EngagementUIModule

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/EngagementUIModule.cs:18` · Level 11 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the Engagement module's UI descriptor. It implements [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule) to contribute six navigation items, one invisible layout component, and its own assembly for Blazor component discovery.
- **Depends on**: [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule), [`NavItem`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#navitem) and [`NavSection`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#navsection) from `MMCA.Common.UI.Common` (`EngagementUIModule.cs:4-5`), [`RoleNames`](group-24-identity-module.md#rolenames) from `MMCA.Common.Shared.Auth` (`:3`), [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths), [`LiveEventListener`](#liveeventlistener) (`:2`), MudBlazor `Icons`, and `System.Reflection`.
- **Concept, the pluggable UI module descriptor.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses how a modular monolith composes its front end without the host hard-coding each module's menu: every module ships an `IUIModule`, and the shared shell merges their nav items, layout components and assemblies. `[Rubric §25, Navigation & IA]`: the menu is declarative data here, not imperative wiring. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: four of the six items carry `RequiredRole: RoleNames.Organizer` (`:27`, `:28`, `:29`, `:31`) so the shared `NavMenu` hides them from attendees; this is menu trimming, and the real enforcement remains the `[Authorize(Roles = "Organizer")]` attribute on each page. `[Rubric §27, i18n]`: per the [ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html) comment (`:20-21`), each `Title` is a resource **key**, resolved by `NavMenu` against the co-located `EngagementUIModule.resx` pair at render time through the `typeof(EngagementUIModule)` title-resource pointer passed to every item, so the labels localize instead of being hard-coded English.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `NavItems` (`:22-32`) is a collection expression of six entries, each pairing a resource key, a centralized route and an icon: `Nav.HappeningNow` to `HappeningNow` (`:26`, `Section: NavSection.User`), `Nav.MyBadge` to `MyBadge` (`:27`, Organizer), `Nav.CheckIn` to `CheckInScan` (`:28`, Organizer), `Nav.Attendance` to `OrganizerAttendance` (`:29`, Organizer), `Nav.MyPoints` to `MyPoints` (`:30`, `Section: NavSection.User`), and `Nav.PointsOverview` to `OrganizerPointsOverview` (`:31`, Organizer). `Nav.HappeningNow` and `Nav.MyPoints` set `Section: NavSection.User`, which places them in the user section beside the profile entry rather than in the main menu. The comment above `HappeningNow` (`:24-25`) gives the reason: the page is `[Authorize]`, and the General section also renders for anonymous visitors, which offered them a link that only ends at the sign-in page.
  - `LayoutComponentTypes` (`:34`): `[typeof(LiveEventListener)]`, an invisible component the shell renders on every page; the class summary says it joins the event live channel while the event is live (`:14-15`).
  - `Assembly` (`:36`): `typeof(EngagementUIModule).Assembly`, so Blazor's router and component discovery can find this module's routable pages.
- **Why it's built this way**: exposing nav, layout components and the assembly as read-only properties lets the host treat every module identically, and keeping the descriptor beside the routes it points at means adding a page is a two-line change in one project. Note what is deliberately absent: the two self-service QR routes (`EngagementRoutePaths.cs:29-32`) contribute no nav item because they are reachable only by scanning a printed code.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton `IUIModule` by [`DependencyInjection`](#dependencyinjection-3)'s `AddEngagementUI` (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:76`); consumed by the shared shell's `NavMenu` and router.

### DependencyInjection

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:22` · Level 12 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the Engagement UI composition root. One static class exposing an `AddEngagementUI()` extension on `IServiceCollection` that registers every Engagement UI service (bookmarks, reminders, feedback, check-in, points, lookups, the live layer, notification scoping) plus the [`EngagementUIModule`](#engagementuimodule) descriptor.
- **Depends on**: `IServiceCollection` and `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Extensions` (for `TryAddSingleton`, `:2`), the Engagement UI service interfaces and implementations, [`ISessionBookmarkUIService`](#isessionbookmarkuiservice) and [`ISessionLiveUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionliveuiservice) from `.Shared` (`:3-4`), and [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule) plus [`INotificationScopeProvider`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#inotificationscopeprovider) from the Common UI framework (`:6-7`).
- **Concept, `extension(IServiceCollection)` registration.** `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` and `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` assess whether a slice owns its own wiring: this is the single place the Engagement UI declares its services, so a host calls `AddEngagementUI()` and stays ignorant of the internals. The C# preview `extension(IServiceCollection services)` block (`DependencyInjection.cs:24`) is the DI-registration idiom used across the codebase (see the primer on [`extension(T)` members](00-primer.md#c-extensiont-types-read-this-once)), which is what lets `AddEngagementUI` read as an instance method on the collection.
- **Walkthrough**: `AddEngagementUI()` (`:23`) registers in labelled groups, all scoped (per circuit) unless noted.
  - Bookmarks (`:26-27`): [`IBookmarkUIService`](#ibookmarkuiservice) to [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice), [`ISessionBookmarkUIService`](#isessionbookmarkuiservice) to [`SessionBookmarkUIService`](#sessionbookmarkuiservice).
  - [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator) (`:31`), registered as a concrete type. The comment ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2, `:29-30`) records that it keeps local notifications in sync with bookmarks and is a pure no-op on hosts whose local-notification service is unsupported.
  - Feedback (`:34-36`): [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice) to [`QuestionLookupService`](#questionlookupservice), [`IEventFeedbackUIService`](#ieventfeedbackuiservice) to [`EventFeedbackService`](#eventfeedbackservice), [`ISessionFeedbackUIService`](#isessionfeedbackuiservice) to [`SessionFeedbackService`](#sessionfeedbackservice).
  - Badge check-in (`:38-40`): [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) to [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice), the one service behind the attendee badge, both organizer write paths and the attendance rollup.
  - Points (`:42-44`): [`IPointsUIService`](#ipointsuiservice) to [`PointsService`](#pointsservice).
  - Cross-module lookups (`:46-48`): [`ISessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#isessionlookupservice) to [`SessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#sessionlookupservice) and [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice) to [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice), the two contracts the check-in surfaces use to name sessions and people that other services own.
  - Live layer (`:50-53`): [`ILivePollUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#ilivepolluiservice), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#iliveeventuiservice), [`INowNextService`](#inownextservice); Wave 2 adds [`ISessionQuestionUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#isessionquestionuiservice) and [`ISessionLiveUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionliveuiservice) (`:55-58`).
  - Notification scoping (`:60-66`): `services.TryAddSingleton(TimeProvider.System)` then a plain `AddScoped<INotificationScopeProvider, CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider>()`. Both lines carry load-bearing comments (`:60-64`): the plain `AddScoped` deliberately **overrides** the null provider that `AddNotificationUI` registers with `TryAddScoped`, so the two calls stay order-independent; and `TimeProvider` is registered here because it drives [`CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider`](#currenteventnotificationscopeprovider)'s cache expiry and no UI head registers it (the heads never call `AddInfrastructure`, which is where the framework's own `TryAddSingleton` lives).
  - Module descriptor (`:68-69`): `AddSingleton<IUIModule, EngagementUIModule>()`, a singleton because the descriptor is immutable metadata, while everything else is scoped since it holds per-circuit state.
  - Returns `services` (`:71`) for fluent chaining.
- **Why it's built this way**: one extension method per module UI keeps registration cohesive and lets a host compose modules by convention. The `TryAdd` versus `Add` choices are the part worth reading closely: `TryAdd` where this module is a *fallback* provider (`TimeProvider`), plain `Add` where it must *win* over a framework default (the notification scope provider). `[Rubric §29, Resilience, Reliability & Business Continuity]`: getting that pair wrong is exactly how the notification bell would silently stop scoping to the current event.
- **Where it's used**: called by the Blazor UI host's startup when the Engagement module is enabled, alongside the other modules' `AddXUI()` calls.

### FeedbackAnswerModel

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/FeedbackAnswerModel.cs:24` · Level 0 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the form model for one answer on the two attendee feedback pages. It is the two-way binding target behind every question field and, more importantly, the single declaration site for the free-text length rule that both pages enforce.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. Externals: `System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations` (`[MaxLength]`). It is consumed through [`ModelValidation`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#modelvalidation)`.ForProperty` and [`DataAnnotationsModelValidator`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#dataannotationsmodelvalidator), both from `MMCA.Common.UI`.
- **Concept introduced, the annotated form model as the one rule declaration.** `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` assesses whether input rules are declared once or restated per field. The naive Blazor shape writes a `MaxLength` on the markup, a `Counter` beside it, and a hand-rolled length check in the submit path: three copies of the same number that drift independently. Here the `[MaxLength]` attribute at `FeedbackAnswerModel.cs:34` is the rule, `AnswerValueMaxLength` (`:31`) is the number, and the markup reads the constant for both the input cap and the character counter (`EventFeedback.razor:72-73`, `SessionFeedback.razor:83-84`). `[Rubric §19, State Management]` covers the other half of its job: each page holds a `Dictionary<QuestionIdentifierType, FeedbackAnswerModel>` of in-progress answers (the `_answers` field in each page's code-behind) rather than editing the DTO it will eventually post, so an unsaved edit never round-trips through a server contract. `[Rubric §27, Internationalization]`: the `ErrorMessage` is not a sentence but a resource key, `"Field.MaxLengthError"`, resolved by each page's own localizing validator ([ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html)), so the shared model produces a per-page translation without the model knowing anything about locales.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `public const int AnswerValueMaxLength = 4000` (`FeedbackAnswerModel.cs:31`) is the client-side mirror of the server invariant. The XML comment names its source, and it checks out: `EventInvariants.AnswerValueMaxLength` is `4000` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Events/EventInvariants.cs:59`) and `SessionInvariants.AnswerValueMaxLength` is the same (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Domain/Sessions/SessionInvariants.cs:37`). The same number also sizes the column (`EventQuestionAnswerConfiguration.cs:26`, `SessionQuestionAnswerConfiguration.cs:26`), so a value the form accepts is a value the table can hold.
  - `TextValue` (`:35`) is the `string?` bound by the plain text question type (the email type no longer binds it directly), and carries the `[MaxLength(AnswerValueMaxLength, ErrorMessage = "Field.MaxLengthError")]` annotation (`:34`).
  - `EmailValue` (`:44-48`) is a second view of the same stored value: its getter and setter read and write `TextValue` (`:46-47`), so only one string is ever stored and submitted. It exists so the EMAIL question type gets its own rules without touching the text rules: `[OptionalEmail(ErrorMessage = "Field.EmailError")]` (`:42`, attribute at `MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Validation/OptionalEmailAttribute.cs:25`) lets a blank answer pass (whether an answer is required stays per-question data) while a non-blank one must be a valid address, and the same `[MaxLength]` (`:43`) still caps the length. The markup binds it for the email case (`EventFeedback.razor:58`, `SessionFeedback.razor:69`).
  - `RatingValue` (`:51`) is a plain `int` with no annotation, because a rating is picked from a five-star control rather than typed. Zero is the unanswered sentinel: `GetAnswerValue` treats `RatingValue > 0` as the only answered state (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:391-392`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:132-133`), so an untouched rating serializes to `null` and is skipped on submit.
  - All three members are settable properties, deliberately. MudBlazor binds with `@bind-SelectedValue` and `@bind-Value` (`EventFeedback.razor:53`, `:58`, `:68`), which needs a writable property, so a `record` with `init`-only members could not serve as the target. The mutability is a consequence of the binding contract, not a lapse from the workspace's `required`/`init` default.
- **Why it's built this way**: the type doc (`FeedbackAnswerModel.cs:6-23`) records the one design tension worth understanding. Both pages render a *dynamic* question list, so whether a given answer is required is per-question data (`QuestionDTO.IsRequired`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/Questions/QuestionDTO.cs:47`), not a property of the model. A `[Required]` attribute here would apply to every question at once and would be wrong for most of them, so required stays on the field's own `Required="@question.IsRequired"` parameter (the `Required` parameter on each field in `EventFeedback.razor`) and only the length rule, which genuinely is per-value, is declared on the model. Lifting the model to a public file-level type shared by both pages, rather than a private nested holder per page, is what makes one declaration serve both forms.
- **Where it's used**: seeded one instance per question in each page's `OnInitializedAsync` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:172`), populated by `PreFillExistingAnswers`, read by `GetAnswerValue` on submit, and reset in place by `DeleteAnswerAsync`. A *separate* prototype instance is constructed in each page's `OnInitialized` purely to build the validation delegates (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:99-101`, `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:88-90`), one for `TextValue` and one for `EmailValue`: `ModelValidation.ForProperty` validates the value handed to it rather than the instance, so one prototype serves the whole dynamic list.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing here enforces the length. The attribute is inert until a validator runs it; the enforcement path is the pages' `_validateAnswerText` and `_validateAnswerEmail` delegates on the client and the Conference invariants on the server.

### CheckInErrorCodes

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckIns/CheckInErrorCodes.cs:8` · Level 0 · static class

- **What it is**: the two stable error codes the self-service QR landing pages branch on, held as constants beside the service that reads them instead of being retyped inside each page.
- **Depends on**: nothing (two `const string` fields). Consumed together with [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), which carries the code back from the transport layer.
- **Concept introduced, the error code as a client-facing contract.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether failures are expressed in a form a client can branch on. HTTP status codes are not enough here and the type doc says why by implication (`CheckInErrorCodes.cs:3-7`): two of the three refusals arrive as the same 404, so a page choosing its state from the status alone cannot tell "nothing is scheduled in this room" from "this feature is off". A stable machine-readable code in the ProblemDetails body is the only distinguishing signal, and it must never be a human-readable message, because that message is localized and would break the branch in any non-English locale. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` covers the placement: one declaration site for the vocabulary means a page never spells a code out inline, so a typo is a compile error rather than a silently unreachable branch.
- **Walkthrough** (`CheckInErrorCodes.cs:8-18`): `EventNotPublished = "CheckIns.EventNotPublished"` (`:11`) means the owning event is not published yet, so nothing is being recorded for it. `NoCurrentSession = "CheckIns.NoCurrentSession"` (`:17`) means nothing is scheduled in the scanned room at this instant, and the member comment (`:13-16`) records the security decision folded into it: an unknown room answers the same way by design, so the state never confirms which room identifiers exist.
- **Why it's built this way**: the deliberate absence is as informative as the presence. There is no constant for "feature disabled" or "unknown target", because those produce a bare 404 with no code, which both pages treat as their generic unavailable state ([`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin)`.razor.cs:70-74`, [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit)`.razor.cs:73-74`). Refusing to name them is what keeps a printed code from confirming which sponsors or rooms exist ([ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html)).
- **Where it's used**: read by [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`:72-73`) and [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`:73`) when mapping an outcome onto their page state; produced on the server as literal strings by [`CheckInProcessor`](#checkinprocessor) (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/CheckIns/Services/CheckInProcessor.cs:30`) and [`RecordRoomCheckInHandler`](#recordroomcheckinhandler) (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/CheckIns/UseCases/RecordRoomCheckIn/RecordRoomCheckInHandler.cs:145`), and documented on the endpoint itself ([`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller)`.cs:150`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the two sides of this contract are separate string literals in separate projects, not one shared constant, because the UI project deliberately does not reference the Application layer. Nothing but a test keeps them equal.

### NowNextSessionInfo

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/INowNextService.cs:27` · Level 0 · sealed record

- **What it is**: one row of the now-and-next snapshot, a session's identifier, title, room name, and its wall-clock start and end in the event's own time zone.
- **Depends on**: the `SessionIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: BCL `DateTime`, plus `System.Text.Json` name-based binding at the call site.
- **Concept, the locally mirrored wire shape.** `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether a module can be lifted out without dragging a neighbor's assembly along, and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` how a payload contract is expressed to its consumers. The bytes on the wire are produced by Conference's [NowNextSessionDTO](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextsessiondto); Engagement redeclares only the fields it renders instead of referencing `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared` for one payload, and JSON binds them by property name. The type doc on the enclosing snapshot records that this is a deliberate mirror and that the Android home-screen widget makes the same call with a mirror of its own (`INowNextService.cs:6-9`; the widget's private copy is at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI/Platforms/Android/NowNextWidgetProvider.cs:130-134`). The same tolerant-reader trade-off taught on [AttendeeRow](#attendeerow) applies: nothing at compile time keeps the mirror aligned with the DTO, only the round-trip tests do.
- **Walkthrough** (`INowNextService.cs:27-32`): five positional members, `SessionId` (line 28, the deep-link target), `Title` (29), `RoomName` (30, nullable), `StartsAtLocal` (31), and `EndsAtLocal` (32). Two details are load-bearing. `RoomName` is null whenever the session carries no room id, or carries one the event's room map does not contain, a decision the server makes in a single expression ([GetNowNextHandler](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownexthandler)`.cs:78`). And the two times are plain `DateTime` wall clock in the event's zone, not UTC and not `DateTimeOffset`: the server passes the stored wall-clock values straight through (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:79-80`) while keeping the UTC instants it computed for its own filtering on its side of the row (`:81-82`), so no client ever converts a zone.
- **Why it's built this way**: glanceable surfaces (a page card, a home-screen widget) should print exactly what the printed schedule says, so the wall-clock value travels as data. The UTC instants stay on the server's side of the contract for callers that do their own math; this mirror deliberately drops them, along with `EventId`. The slice arrives with [ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 8 (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:13`).
- **Where it's used**: only inside [NowNextSnapshot](#nownextsnapshot)'s `Now` and `Next` lists; the [HappeningNow](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page holds them in `_sessionsNow` / `_sessionsNext` (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:55-56`) and renders one card per row.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the mirror is checked by [NowNextServiceTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) round-tripping a JSON body, not by the compiler, so a renamed server property would deserialize to a default value rather than break the build.

### SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckIns/SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:25` · Level 0 · sealed record (generic)

- **What it is**: the return shape of one self-service check-in post, a sponsor booth visit or a room check-in. It carries the recorded payload, the server's stable error code, or a flag saying the attempt got no definitive answer at all, so the calling page can pick its state without catching an exception.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; it is closed over [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto) and [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto) at the two call sites, and its `ErrorCode` values come from [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes). Externals: BCL only, with a `where TResult : class` constraint (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:26`) so the nullable `Value` means exactly "absent".
- **Concept introduced, the result envelope for a refusal that is not a fault.** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` both apply: the type doc (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:3-13`) explains that the landing pages must tell three server answers apart (no session running in this room, event not published, and the flat "unavailable" a disabled feature or unknown target produces), that all three arrive as ordinary non-success responses, and that **two of them share the same 404 status**. A result carrying only a message would force the page to parse localized prose; an exception would force a `try`/`catch` around a normal outcome. Carrying the code as data is what makes the page's mapping a `switch` expression over constants. The envelope also keeps a refusal apart from a non-answer: a rate limit, a timeout, a 5xx, a dropped network or a sign-in that could not be refreshed is not the server saying no, so it travels as `IsTransient` (param doc at `SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:20-24`) and the page offers a Retry instead of telling the attendee the code is not available. Note this is deliberately **not** the framework's [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), which the other four members of the same interface do return: this is a UI-layer transport envelope for one narrow HTTP pattern, so it stays small and local rather than reusing an abstraction whose error vocabulary it does not need.
- **Walkthrough** (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:25-29`): three positional members, `Value` (the recorded outcome, null when the server refused), `ErrorCode` (null when the response carried none: a bare feature-gate 404 or an unparseable body, per the param doc at `:16-19`) and `IsTransient`, which defaults to `false` (`:25`) so every refusal construction stays a two-argument call and only the no-answer branches name the flag. One computed member, `IsSuccess => Value is not null` (`:29`), so success is derived from the payload rather than tracked as a further field that could disagree with it. Construction is centralized in [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice)`.PostSelfCheckInAsync` (`CheckInService.cs:165-201`): the success branch deserializes `TResult` and pairs it with a null code (`:178-182`); a 401 still standing after the refresh attempt, or a retryable status still standing after the in-place retries, returns `IsTransient: true` (`:184-187`), as do a transport failure (`:191-194`) and an `HttpClient` timeout, which surfaces as a cancellation the caller never requested (`:195-200`); any other non-success passes null with whatever `ReadErrorCodeAsync` extracted from the RFC 9457 problem body (`:189`, defined at `:393-428`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the four other members on the same service run through `HttpResultExecutor.ExecuteAsync` and answer with a framework [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) (`CheckInService.cs:56-71`, `:108-124`, `:131-146`), because an organizer facing a broken scan wants a typed failure to surface. The two attendee-facing self-service writes must not, because every refusal is a sentence the landing page renders calmly to someone holding a phone in a hallway, and an unanswered attempt is a Retry button rather than a refusal. The method doc records both rules (`CheckInService.cs:148-164`). Keeping the generic on the payload lets one private helper serve both endpoints with no duplication ([ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html)).
- **Where it's used**: returned by [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice)`.RecordSponsorVisitAsync` and `.RecordRoomCheckInAsync` (`ICheckInUIService.cs:58`, `:70`), implemented at `CheckInService.cs:86` and `:97`, consumed by [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:89`, mapping `IsTransient` to [`VisitState`](#visitstate)`.Transient` at `:112`) and [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:87`, mapping it to [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate)`.Transient` at `:110`). Page-tested through [`SponsorVisitTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) and [`RoomCheckInTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).

### SessionReminder

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/SessionReminderPlanner.cs:15` · Level 0 · sealed record

- **What it is**: an immutable value describing one planned on-device reminder for a bookmarked session, a stable notification id, when it should fire, and where a tap takes the user.
- **Depends on**: the `SessionIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: BCL `DateTimeOffset`. No first-party types beyond the alias.
- **Concept, the immutable planning DTO.** `[Rubric §19, State Management]` assesses whether client-side state is modeled as explicit, predictable values rather than mutable ad-hoc fields. A `sealed record` gives value equality and `init`-only positional members for free (see [primer §4](00-primer.md#4-c-build-and-code-style-conventions)), so a planned reminder is a snapshot that cannot drift after [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner) computes it. The type carries no behavior: it is pure data handed from the pure planner to the stateful coordinator.
- **Walkthrough** (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:15-29`): six positional members, `NotificationId` (int, line 16), `SessionId` (17), `SessionTitle` (18), `StartsAtUtc` (19), `DeliverAt` (20), and `DeepLinkRoute` (21), plus one `init`-only body property, `IsImmediate` (`:28`). The doc comment (`:5-14`) is load-bearing intent: `NotificationId` is derived deterministically from the session id so rescheduling **replaces** an existing OS notification instead of stacking a duplicate, `DeliverAt` is the fire instant (start minus lead, clamped to now), and `DeepLinkRoute` is the app-relative route published on tap. `IsImmediate` (doc at `:23-27`) marks a reminder whose session was already inside the lead window at planning time, so its `DeliverAt` was clamped to fire almost at once; the doc states the contract the coordinator enforces, such a reminder fires once, when the session becomes tracked, not again on every replan. It is a body property rather than a seventh positional member, so existing positional construction stays valid and the planner sets it with an object initializer.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the planned reminder as a value lets the planner stay a pure function and the coordinator diff plans by id ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2, the local-notification dispatcher). Carrying `IsImmediate` on the value, rather than having the coordinator recompute "was this clamped", keeps the window decision in the one place that did the date math.
- **Where it's used**: produced by [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner)`.Plan` (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:90-99`); consumed by [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator), which calls `Plan` at `SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:175`, reads `IsImmediate` to skip an already-issued immediate reminder (`:215`), and turns each remaining value into a [`LocalNotificationRequest`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#localnotificationrequest) (`:223`).

### NowNextSnapshot

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/INowNextService.cs:15` · Level 1 · sealed record

- **What it is**: the whole "happening now / up next" payload for one event as the Engagement UI consumes it, the event's display name, whether the event is live at the query instant, and two lists of [NowNextSessionInfo](#nownextsessioninfo) rows.
- **Depends on**: [NowNextSessionInfo](#nownextsessioninfo). Externals: BCL `IReadOnlyList<T>`.
- **Concept, the two-bucket time projection, computed once on the server.** `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether work sits where it is cheapest, and `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` whether a page renders a prepared view model rather than deriving one. Both buckets are decided by [GetNowNextHandler](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownexthandler) against a single instant taken from an injected `TimeProvider` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:29`), never from the browser clock. `Now` is every eligible session whose window is half-open around that instant, `StartsAtUtc <= utcNow && utcNow < EndsAtUtc` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:53`), so a session that ends exactly now is already out and the one starting exactly now is already in. `Next` is not "the next session": it is the batch that shares the earliest future start (`:58-66`), which is what makes parallel tracks appear together instead of one arbitrary room winning. The empty cases are ordinary, not error states: between sessions `Now` is empty, after the last session `Next` is empty (`nextStart` is null and the list is `[]`, `:60-62`), and before the first session `Now` is empty while `Next` carries the opening batch.
- **Walkthrough** (`INowNextService.cs:15-19`): four positional members, `EventName` (line 16), `IsLive` (17), `Now` (18), and `Next` (19). `IsLive` is independent of the two lists: the server compares the same instant against the event's live window from [CurrentEventSelector](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector)`.GetLiveWindowUtc(StartDate, EndDate, TimeZone)` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:68-69`), so a live event during a coffee break reports `IsLive: true` with an empty `Now`. Ordering is fixed server-side too: `Now` sorts by start then by room name ordinal-ignore-case (`:54-55`), `Next` by room name alone (`:65`) since every row in it shares one start.
- **Why it's built this way**: no client can compute either bucket correctly on its own, because eligibility depends on data a public catalog read does not expose (see [INowNextService](#inownextservice)), so the snapshot is shipped precomputed and every surface (page and widget) shows the same answer. The one snapshot type also keeps `IsLive` next to the lists, which is what lets a caller distinguish "the conference is not running" from "nothing is on right now".
- **Where it's used**: carried by the [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) that [INowNextService](#inownextservice)`.GetAsync` returns; the [HappeningNow](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page projects it with `result.Value?.Now ?? []` and `result.Value?.Next ?? []` (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:190-192`), so a failed read renders the same empty state as an empty list. Conference's own wire type is [NowNextDTO](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextdto) (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:71`), and the Android widget deserializes a third copy of the shape ([NowNextWidgetProvider](group-25-adc-host-composition.md#nownextwidgetprovider)`.cs:127`, `:132`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing on the record marks how fresh it is. Staleness is bounded by one cache only, the endpoint's 60-second `NowNextCache` output-cache policy (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:293`, applied at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventsController.cs:178` and `:193`); [GetNowNextQuery](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownextquery) deliberately has no query-level cache under it (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Application/Sessions/UseCases/NowNext/GetNowNextQuery.cs:10-12`). The page fetches the snapshot from its own load path (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:190`) and has no timer of its own for it, so a long-open tab keeps its first now-and-next lists until something refreshes it.

### SessionReminderPlanner

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/SessionReminderPlanner.cs:39` · Level 1 · static class

- **What it is**: a pure, side-effect-free planner that turns a set of bookmarked sessions plus an event time zone and a lead time into a list of [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder) values. All the date math (wall-clock to UTC, DST edge cases, lead-window clamping) lives here.
- **Depends on**: [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder), [`SessionInfo`](group-22-engagement-module.md#sessioninfo) (the input session shape), [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths) (the tap route), and the `SessionIdentifierType` alias. Externals: BCL `TimeZoneInfo`, `DateTimeOffset`.
- **Concept, the deterministic pure function.** `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether logic can be exercised without infrastructure. Every input the planner needs is a parameter, including `now` (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:61`), the current instant, which the caller supplies rather than the planner reading `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` itself. That single choice makes DST behavior, lead-window clamping and skip rules unit-testable with fixed clocks and no OS notification stack. `[Rubric §27, Internationalization]` also applies: session times are wall-clock local to the event's IANA time zone, never UTC in the DTOs (`SessionInfo.StartsAt` is a bare `DateTime?`, `ISessionLookupService.cs:12`), so correct conversion is a first-class concern here rather than an afterthought.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `NotificationIdSeed` (`:44`) is a private constant offset that keeps reminder ids clear of any other local-notification ids the app might use. The comment above it (`:41-43`) states the assumption the whole replace-by-id contract rests on: `SessionIdentifierType` hash codes are stable for value types.
  - `GetNotificationId(sessionId)` (`:47-48`) shifts the seed 16 bits and XORs the session id's hash `unchecked`, producing that stable id.
  - `Plan(bookmarkedSessions, timeZoneId, leadTime, now)` (`:57-103`) guards its arguments (`:63-64`), resolves the `TimeZoneInfo` (`:66`), then per session skips those with no start time (`:71-74`) or already started (`:77-80`), computes `deliverAt = startsAtUtc - leadTime` (`:82`) and captures `isImmediate = deliverAt <= now` (`:83`); when that holds it fires the reminder 30 seconds out rather than dropping it (`:84-88`). Finally it emits a [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder) whose tap route comes from [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.SessionDetails` (`:90-96`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/EngagementRoutePaths.cs:12`), with `IsImmediate = isImmediate` set by object initializer (`:97-99`). The planner only records the clamp; deciding whether a clamped reminder should actually be scheduled again is the coordinator's job.
  - `ToUtc(localWallClock, timeZone)` (`:105-118`) is the DST-correct converter: it marks the wall time `Unspecified` (`:107`), shifts a spring-forward **invalid** time forward one hour (`:110-113`), and lets `GetUtcOffset` resolve ambiguous fall-back times to the standard offset (`:115-117`).
- **Why it's built this way**: separating planning (pure, here) from scheduling (I/O, in the coordinator) means the tricky calendar logic is provable in isolation and the coordinator stays a thin best-effort orchestrator ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2, named in the type doc at `:32`).
- **Where it's used**: called by [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator)`.ReplanAsync` (`SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:175`); `GetNotificationId` is also called there to cancel reminders for sessions that left the tracked set (`:109`), to cancel everything when reminders are switched off (`:138`), and to sweep the ids that dropped out of the plan (`:190`). Covered by [SessionReminderPlannerTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).

### SessionFeedbackAnswers

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:14` · Level 2 · internal sealed class

- **What it is**: the answer state of the session-feedback form, lifted out of the page's code-behind into a plain class: the ordered questions, the per-question bound answer models, the ids of answers the reader already saved, and the set of required questions left unanswered. The page holds one instance (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:65`) and delegates every state transition to it.
- **Depends on**: [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`SessionQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionquestionanswerdto), and the `QuestionIdentifierType`, `SessionIdentifierType` and `SessionQuestionAnswerIdentifierType` aliases. Externals: `System.Globalization` (invariant `int` parse and format), LINQ.
- **Concept introduced, form state as a testable object.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses component/logic separation, and `[Rubric §19, State Management]` whether state transitions can be reasoned about apart from the render tree. The page used to keep the questions, the answers map, the saved-answer ids and the missing-required set as private fields beside its lifecycle code; they now live here (`SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:16-17`, `:20`, `:23`), so the rules that decide what the form shows, pre-fills, flags and submits are a class with no Blazor dependency. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` is the rule the class carries: `MissingRequired` (`:33`) exists because MudRating is not a form input, so MudForm validation cannot block an unanswered required rating; this set does, and the page binds each question's inline error to it (doc `:28-32`).
- **Walkthrough** (state first, then the transitions in the order the page calls them)
  - State: `Questions` (`:20`) and `Answers` (`:23`, a map from question id to [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel)) have private setters; `HasExistingFeedback` (`:26`) is true once at least one saved-answer id is recorded, which the page uses to label its button "Update" or "Submit". `HasSavedAnswer` (`:38`) and `TryGetSavedAnswerId` (`:44`) read the private saved-id map so the page can offer to clear a saved answer without owning that map.
  - `SetQuestions` (`:49-53`) orders the questions by `Sort` and starts every answer blank, one fresh model per question.
  - `PreFill` (`:58-86`) maps the reader's saved answers onto those models. It is defense in depth: feedback questions are shared across sessions, so an answer from another session would match by `QuestionId`, and the loop skips any answer whose `SessionId` differs from the form's (`:64`). An answer with no matching question is skipped (`:69`). Otherwise it records the answer id (`:74`) and, for a `Rating` question whose value parses as an invariant `int`, sets `RatingValue` (`:76-80`); everything else goes to `TextValue` (`:83`).
  - `FlagMissingRequiredAnswers` (`:93-103`) clears the set, then adds every question that `IsRequired` and whose answer reads blank, for every question type, and returns whether any were found. Going through the private `GetAnswerValue` is what makes it type-agnostic: an unrated rating (0) reads as blank exactly like an empty text field.
  - `PendingAnswers` (`:107-111`) projects the answered questions into the `(QuestionId, AnswerValue)` list for the one POST-as-upsert, dropping blanks, so an all-optional untouched form yields an empty list.
  - `ClearSavedAnswer` (`:115-123`) forgets one saved id after the server deleted the answer and blanks that field (`TextValue` to null, `RatingValue` to 0).
  - `GetAnswerValue` (`:125-135`) is the bridge from a model back to a wire string: `TextValue` for every non-rating type, and `RatingValue` as an invariant string only when above 0.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class is `internal` and sealed because its only consumer is the page in the same assembly, and it takes the session id as a `PreFill` argument rather than storing it, so one instance carries no hidden dependency on a route. Replacing the whole instance (`_answerState = new()`, `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:120`) is how the page discards the previous session's form when the route id changes, instead of clearing four collections one by one.
- **Where it's used**: only by [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback) (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:65`). [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) does not use it: it keeps the same logic inline against `EventQuestionAnswerDTO`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: no test class named for this type was confirmed in this pass, so coverage is by the page's component tests ([`SessionFeedbackTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup)) only as far as they drive the page.

### ICheckInUIService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckIns/ICheckInUIService.cs:20` · Level 3 · interface

- **What it is**: the single UI contract for every QR badge check-in surface: the attendee's own badge, the organizer scan and manual fallback writes, the two attendee self-service scans, and the organizer attendance rollup.
- **Depends on**: [`MyBadgeDTO`](#mybadgedto), [`CheckInAttendeeRequest`](#checkinattendeerequest), [`ManualCheckInRequest`](#manualcheckinrequest), [`CheckInResultDTO`](#checkinresultdto), [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto), [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto), [`AttendanceStatsDTO`](#attendancestatsdto), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), and the `SponsorIdentifierType` / `RoomIdentifierType` / `EventIdentifierType` aliases. The UI service abstraction itself is taught on [`IBookmarkUIService`](#ibookmarkuiservice).
- **Concept introduced, two error contracts in one interface.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a contract's failure semantics are legible from its signatures, and this interface is the clearest example in the module because it deliberately carries **two**. Four methods return a [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), the ordinary shape for this module: a refusal is a typed failure the page renders. Two methods return [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult) instead, because those two are the endpoints an attendee reaches by scanning a printed code, and every refusal there has to be told apart by a stable error CODE and rendered as a calm sentence. The type doc states exactly that split (`ICheckInUIService.cs:13-18`). The same two methods are also the only ones that take an idempotency key, because they are the only ones the client retries on its own. The class doc on [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice) (`CheckInService.cs:17-20`) states the other axis: every call is authenticated, the badge read is caller-scoped ("my badge"), and the three organizer calls are role-gated server-side, so `[Rubric §11, Security]` lives on the server, not in this contract.
- **Walkthrough** (`ICheckInUIService.cs:20-83`):
  - `GetMyBadgeAsync(ct)` (`:27`) fetches the signed-in attendee's own badge credential; an attendee with no badge answers `ErrorType.NotFound`, which the badge page renders as its empty state (`:22-26`).
  - `CheckInByCredentialAsync(request, ct)` (`:34-36`) checks an attendee in from a scanned or typed badge payload; organizer only (`:30`).
  - `ManualCheckInAsync(request, ct)` (`:44-46`) is the fallback used when a badge cannot be scanned; organizer only. Both post through the shared `PostCheckInAsync` (`CheckInService.cs:131`), whose doc (`:126-130`) records that a repeat check-in is reported through `AlreadyCheckedIn` rather than as a failure, so there is no conflict branch to interpret.
  - `RecordSponsorVisitAsync(sponsorId, idempotencyKey, ct)` (`:58-61`) records the caller's visit to one sponsor booth; the doc states a repeat visit is a success carrying `AlreadyVisited`, not a failure (`:50`).
  - `RecordRoomCheckInAsync(roomId, idempotencyKey, ct)` (`:70-73`) checks the caller into whatever session the scanned room is hosting right now. The doc sentence at `:64-65` is the whole design of that surface: **the session is never sent**, the server resolves it from the room and the configured grace window, which is why a printed room code never expires when the schedule moves.
  - The `idempotencyKey` on both self-service writes (param docs at `:53-56` and `:68`) is one key per attendee attempt, reused by every retry of it, the page's Retry included, so the server's idempotency filter replays the first answer instead of reporting a second visit. The pages mint it with `Guid.NewGuid().ToString("N")` (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:75`, `RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:73`), and both server endpoints carry `[Idempotent]` (`CheckInsController.cs:133`, `:168`).
  - `GetAttendanceStatsAsync(eventId, ct)` (`:80-82`) fetches the attendance rollup for one event; organizer only (`:76`, enforced by `[HasPermission]` at `CheckInsController.cs:187`).
- **Why it's built this way**: one interface rather than one per surface keeps the whole check-in vocabulary, and the single `checkins` resource it maps to (`CheckInService.cs:32`), in one reviewable place; the six methods correspond one-for-one to the six endpoints on [`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller) ([ADR-071](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/071-barcode-scanning-and-qr-display.html) for the scan surfaces, [ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html) for the check-in and points model).
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice) (`CheckInService.cs:27`), registered scoped (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:47`), and injected into five pages: [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:26`), [`MyBadge`](#mybadge) (`MyBadge.razor.cs:17`), [`OrganizerAttendance`](#organizerattendance) (`OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:20`), [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:26`) and [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:29`). Server-side the whole controller sits behind `[FeatureGate(EngagementFeatures.CheckIn)]` (`CheckInsController.cs:37`) with the two self-service writes gated again individually (`:134`, `:169`), see [`EngagementFeatures`](#engagementfeatures) and [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html).

### INowNextService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/INowNextService.cs:40` · Level 3 · interface

- **What it is**: the UI-side contract for reading one event's now-and-next snapshot, a single method returning a [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) over a [NowNextSnapshot](#nownextsnapshot).
- **Depends on**: [NowNextSnapshot](#nownextsnapshot), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), and the `EventIdentifierType` alias. Externals: BCL `Task`, `CancellationToken`.
- **Concept, a port over a server-owned computation.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses whether components depend on typed contracts rather than transport, and `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` whether cross-module reads travel through an explicit boundary. The interface doc (`INowNextService.cs:34-39`) is the contract worth memorizing, because it enumerates what the **server** owns and the client therefore must not re-derive: session eligibility (scheduled, non-service, not declined or cancelled), the event-local wall-clock conversion, and the "next = the batch sharing the earliest future start" rule. Concretely, eligibility is [`CalendarExportMapper`](group-18-conference-application.md#calendarexportmapper)`.IsExportable` ([GetNowNextHandler](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownexthandler)`.cs:48`). None of that is visible in a public catalog listing, which is precisely why the page cannot compute now-and-next itself; the implementation's doc records that this endpoint replaces the old whole-catalog fetch on Happening Now (`NowNextService.cs:8-12`).
- **Walkthrough** (`INowNextService.cs:49-51`): one method, `GetAsync(eventId, cancellationToken = default)`, returning `Task<Result<NowNextSnapshot>>`. The failure contract is the doc at `:42-46`: an event that is not found or not published answers an `ErrorType.NotFound` failure, which the page renders as "nothing scheduled". The server produces it as `Result.Failure` carrying `Error.NotFound` when the event is missing or `IsPublished` is false (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:32-36`), the API surfaces it as a 404, and [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader) turns it back into a typed failure in the client (`NowNextService.cs:31-33`). Answering with a failure rather than throwing is what lets a caller degrade to an empty schedule with no `try`/`catch` of its own.
- **Why it's built this way**: the one-method port is the DI swap point (`services.AddScoped<INowNextService, NowNextService>()`, `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:60`) and the test extension point, so the page can be exercised against a fake snapshot with no HTTP stack; it also keeps Engagement free of any compile-time reference to Conference for this read ([ADR-007](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/007-grpc-extraction.html) and [ADR-008](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/008-service-extraction-topology.html) put the module in its own process, so this crosses a real service boundary over public HTTP; the implementation uses the plain `"APIClient"` factory client with no token, `NowNextService.cs:23`, because the endpoint is anonymous).
- **Where it's used**: injected into the [HappeningNow](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:28`) and called from its load path (`:156`). Implemented by [NowNextService](#nownextservice) (`NowNextService.cs:14`); covered by [NowNextServiceTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the contract exposes only the per-event form. The endpoint also serves an id-less form where the server auto-selects the current-or-next published event, but no first-party C# client for it exists in this UI; the Android widget calls `Events/now-next` with a bare `HttpClient` instead ([NowNextWidgetProvider](group-25-adc-host-composition.md#nownextwidgetprovider)`.cs:119-120`). Note also a wording gap: the doc comment says "not declined or cancelled" while the code's actual gate is the calendar-export allow-list above.
