### CheckInService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.CheckIns` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckIns/CheckInService.cs:27` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the single HTTP client for every badge and scan surface in the module, implementing [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) against the `checkins` resource (`CheckInService.cs:32`). It carries six calls with three different audiences: the attendee reading their own badge, the organizer scanning or manually checking someone in, and the attendee self-recording a sponsor booth or room visit. The two self-service writes also carry their own bounded retry, token-refresh replay and idempotency key, because the attendee is standing at the booth or the door when they fire.
- **Depends on**: [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice), [`AuthenticatedServiceBase`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#authenticatedservicebase), [`ITokenStorageService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itokenstorageservice), [`ITokenRefresher`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itokenrefresher) (optional, `CheckInService.cs:30`), [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor), [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader), [`IdempotencyHeaders`](group-08-auth.md#idempotencyheaders), [`MyBadgeDTO`](#mybadgedto), [`CheckInAttendeeRequest`](#checkinattendeerequest), [`ManualCheckInRequest`](#manualcheckinrequest), [`CheckInResultDTO`](#checkinresultdto), [`SponsorVisitRequest`](#sponsorvisitrequest), [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto), [`RoomCheckInRequest`](#roomcheckinrequest), [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto), [`AttendanceStatsDTO`](#attendancestatsdto), [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult). Externals: `IHttpClientFactory`, `System.Net` (`HttpStatusCode`), `System.Text.Json` (`JsonDocument`), `System.Net.Http.Json` (`JsonContent`, `ReadFromJsonAsync`), `System.Globalization`, and the `System.Threading.Lock` behind the single-flight refresh.
- **Concept introduced, two failure dialects in one client.** The authenticated-HTTP shape is taught at [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice); what is new here is that this class deliberately reports failure two different ways depending on who is looking at the screen. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a client contract preserves the distinctions the server actually makes. The four organizer and badge calls answer with a [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), carrying the server's own error code and type. The two attendee self-service calls do not: they return a [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult) carrying only a raw error-code string, because the QR landing pages have to tell "no session is running in this room" from "the owning event is not published" and **both arrive as HTTP 404** (`CheckInService.cs:148-164`, and the outcome record's own doc at `SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:3-13`). A status code alone cannot pick the state, so the code is read out of the problem body and handed to the page. The same doc adds a third answer (`:155-163`): an attempt that got no definitive answer is **not** a refusal. A 408, 409 (the same key still in flight), 429 or 5xx is retried in place, a 401 is refreshed and replayed once, and whatever is still unanswered comes back as `IsTransient`, which the page offers a Retry for; only the caller's own cancellation propagates. `[Rubric §26, Front-End Security]` is the reason the pages then collapse several of the refusal codes into one message: see [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate) and [`VisitState`](#visitstate).
- **Walkthrough** (public calls first, then the self-service send pipeline)
  - The primary constructor (`:27-30`) takes the factory, the token storage and an optional `ITokenRefresher` defaulting to `null`; the param doc (`:23-26`) records that a host registering no refresher reports a 401 on a self check-in as transient instead of replaying it. `SelfCheckInMaxAttempts = 3` (`:38`, the first send plus two retries, kept small because the page's Retry covers anything longer) and `MaxRetryAfter` of 5 seconds (`:44`) bound the in-place retries. `_refreshSync` and `_refreshInFlight` (`:46-47`) hold the shared refresh, and the internal settable `Delay` delegate (`:53`) lets a test run the retries without waiting out the real delays.
  - `GetMyBadgeAsync` (`:56-71`) GETs `checkins/my-badge`. The comment (`:66-68`) records the contract precisely: a 404 arrives as a `NotFound` **failure** rather than the old `null`, so "this attendee has no badge" stays a distinguishable state while the badge page renders exactly the empty surface it rendered for `null`. The server mints a badge on first use, so this read is a create-or-get on the other side (`CheckInsController.cs:50`).
  - `CheckInByCredentialAsync` (`:74-77`) and `ManualCheckInAsync` (`:80-83`) are one-line delegations to `PostCheckInAsync`, differing only in the relative URL: the root `checkins` and `checkins/manual`. Both server endpoints are permission-gated on `EngagementPermissions.CheckInManage` (`CheckInsController.cs:79`, `:103`), so the client does no role check of its own.
  - `RecordSponsorVisitAsync` (`:86-94`) and `RecordRoomCheckInAsync` (`:97-105`) build their one-field requests inline ([`SponsorVisitRequest`](#sponsorvisitrequest), [`RoomCheckInRequest`](#roomcheckinrequest)) and delegate to `PostSelfCheckInAsync` together with the caller's `idempotencyKey`. Neither call passes an attendee identity: the server takes it from the token, so a scanned code can only ever check in the person holding the phone. Each also has its own feature gate on the server (`CheckInsController.cs:134`, `:169`).
  - `GetAttendanceStatsAsync` (`:108-124`) GETs `checkins/stats?eventId=` with invariant formatting (`:116`) and returns the [`AttendanceStatsDTO`](#attendancestatsdto) rollup; the endpoint is permission-gated the same way as the writes (`CheckInsController.cs:187`).
  - `PostCheckInAsync<TRequest>` (`:131-146`) is the `Result`-returning poster shared by the two organizer writes. Its doc comment (`:126-130`) records why there is no conflict branch: a repeat check-in comes back with `AlreadyCheckedIn` set rather than as a 409, so the caller has one success shape to interpret.
  - `PostSelfCheckInAsync<TRequest, TResult>` (`:165-201`) is the outcome-returning poster, and notably the one path in the class that does **not** go through [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor) or the reader. It rejects a blank key up front (`:172`), sends through `SendSelfCheckInAsync`, and on success reads the payload with `ReadFromJsonAsync` and wraps it (`:178-182`). A 401 or a retryable status that is still standing becomes `IsTransient: true` (`:184-187`); any other non-success becomes `new SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>(null, await ReadErrorCodeAsync(...))` (`:189`), so a refusal is data, not an exception. An `HttpRequestException` (`:191-194`) and an `OperationCanceledException` the caller did not ask for, which is how `HttpClient.Timeout` surfaces (`:195-200`), are both transient too.
  - `SendSelfCheckInAsync` (`:209-236`) runs the retrying send on an authenticated client (`:216-219`) and returns that response unless it is a 401 with a refresher available (`:221-224`). Then it acquires a refreshed token (`:226-230`), disposes the rejected response and replays the write once on a client built with the new token (`:232-235`). The doc (`:203-208`) explains why storage is not simply re-read: the stored token can still look fresh by the client clock after the server rejected it.
  - `SendWithRetriesAsync` (`:243-271`) loops until it gets a definitive answer or runs out of attempts, with the backoff starting at one second (`:251`) and doubling after each wait (`:269`). Only the last attempt lets a transport failure propagate (`throwOnTransportFailure: retriesLeft == 0`, `:256`); earlier ones come back as a null response and wait the current backoff (`:260`).
  - `TrySendOnceAsync` (`:276-301`) builds a fresh `HttpRequestMessage` per attempt, since a sent message cannot be sent again (`:284-290`), and stamps every one with the same `IdempotencyHeaders.IdempotencyKey` header (`:291`).
  - `RetryWait` (`:308-317`) answers null, meaning final, on the last attempt or a non-retryable status, and otherwise prefers the server's `Retry-After` over the backoff; a requested wait past `MaxRetryAfter` also ends the attempt (`:316`) rather than holding the page on its loading state. `IsRetryableStatus` (`:324-327`) is 408, 409, 429, or any 5xx except 501 and 505, and `RetryAfter` (`:330-345`) reads either header form, clamped at zero.
  - `TryAcquireRefreshedTokenAsync` (`:353-367`) is single-flight under `_refreshSync`: two check-ins landing on one rejected token share one rotation, because on MAUI a second rotation presenting the same refresh token is read as reuse and revokes the session (BR-206, per the doc at `:347-352`). Each caller waits on the shared task with its own token (`:366`), while `AcquireRefreshedTokenAsync` (`:369-386`) runs under `CancellationToken.None` (`:373`), so one caller giving up never cancels the refresh for another. A blank token, an `InvalidOperationException` (no JS interop during SSR prerender) or an `HttpRequestException` yields `null` (`:374-385`), which leaves the 401 standing and the outcome transient.
  - `ReadErrorCodeAsync` (`:393-428`) walks the problem body's `errors` array and returns the first element's `code` string (`:410-418`). Everything else yields `null`: an empty body (`:400-401`), a body with no `errors` array (`:404-408`), a non-string code, or a `JsonException` from an HTML error page or a bare challenge (`:422-427`, with the comment naming that case). `null` is a meaningful value here, it is what the pages render as the generic "unavailable" state.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class doc (`:17-20`) sets the rule that every call is authenticated, including the badge read, because the badge is caller-scoped rather than public (`CheckInsController.cs:50`). The split between the two posters is the client-side consequence of a server design decision recorded in [ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html): organizer scanning and attendee self-service produce the same domain writes but face very different humans, so the refusal vocabulary is stable ([`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes)) and the client passes it through untranslated. Retrying a write is only safe because every attempt carries the same key and the server endpoints are `[Idempotent]` (`CheckInsController.cs:133`, `:168`): a replayed first answer can never be counted as a second visit.
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped as [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:47`) and injected by five surfaces: [`MyBadge`](#mybadge) (`MyBadge.razor.cs:17`, called at `:63`), [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:26`, called at `:254`, `:277`), [`OrganizerAttendance`](#organizerattendance) (`OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:20`, called at `:142`), [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:26`, called at `:87`) and [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:29`, called at `:89`). The whole controller sits behind the `EngagementFeatures.CheckIn` gate (`CheckInsController.cs:37`), so a deployment with the feature off answers 404 to every one of these calls.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: [`CheckInServiceTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) covers only the self-service path: response disposal on success and failure (`:38`, `:66`), the idempotency key on both writes (`:87`, `:99`), a 429 honoring `Retry-After` under the same key (`:118`), the bounded retry ending transient (`:138`), a `Retry-After` past the cap (`:153`), the 401 refresh-and-replay and its unrefreshable case (`:166`, `:184`), repeated transport failure and an `HttpClient` timeout (`:199`, `:211`), and caller cancellation propagating (`:222`). The other four calls are exercised indirectly through the bUnit page tests ([`MyBadgeTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), [`CheckInScanTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), [`OrganizerAttendanceTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), [`RoomCheckInTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), [`SponsorVisitTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup)), which mock the interface rather than the transport. Which error codes a given server build emits is the API's contract, not this file's.

### NowNextService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/NowNextService.cs:14` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the HTTP implementation of [`INowNextService`](#inownextservice), one GET against the Conference API's anonymous `Events/{id}/now-next` endpoint, read into a [`NowNextSnapshot`](#nownextsnapshot) and handed back untouched.
- **Depends on**: [`INowNextService`](#inownextservice), [`NowNextSnapshot`](#nownextsnapshot), [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor), [`IdempotentReadRetry`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#idempotentreadretry), [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader). Externals: `IHttpClientFactory`, `HttpClient`, `CultureInfo`.
- **Concept introduced, the thin lookup service** (the contrast with the authenticated services above). `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` and `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]`. Unlike [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice) this class does not derive from [`AuthenticatedServiceBase`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#authenticatedservicebase): it takes the named `"APIClient"` from the factory directly (`NowNextService.cs:23`), the same shape as the module's other read-only lookups ([`SessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#sessionlookupservice)). That named client is registered once in the framework (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:126`) with the gateway base address plus the auth and culture delegating handlers, so a bearer token still rides along when the user has one, but the endpoint is `[AllowAnonymous]` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/Events/EventsController.cs:177`) and an anonymous visitor gets the same payload. The consequence to notice: no token fetch wraps this call, because there is nothing user-specific to authorize. Retry still applies, without the base class: the GET goes through [`IdempotentReadRetry`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#idempotentreadretry)`.GetAsync` (`:26-29`), whose policy is the same object `AuthenticatedServiceBase` uses (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/IdempotentReadRetry.cs:24`), so a transient failure (an `HttpRequestException`, a 5xx other than 501/505, 408 or 429) is re-sent up to three times with exponential backoff plus jitter (`IdempotentReadRetry.cs:10-12`). The inline comment names why that is safe here (`NowNextService.cs:25`): the read is idempotent and anonymous, like the other lookups.
- **Walkthrough** (`NowNextService.cs:17-35`), which is mostly guards
  - The whole body runs inside [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor)`.ExecuteAsync` (`:20`), so a refused connection or a broken socket becomes a `Http.TransportFailure` failure rather than an exception the page has to catch.
  - Both disposables are scoped with `using`, the client (`:23`) and the final response (`:26`), so a per-call fetch leaks neither; any response a retry discards is disposed by the policy itself (`IdempotentReadRetry.cs:27-28`).
  - The relative URI is built with `string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, $"Events/{eventId}/now-next")` (`:28`). Invariant formatting is not decoration: the id is interpolated into a *path*, and a culture-sensitive numeric format would produce a path the API cannot route.
  - The response goes through [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader)`.ReadAsync<NowNextSnapshot>` (`:33`), so **404 becomes a `NotFound` failure**, with the comment naming the case it covers (an unpublished or deleted event) and the consequence: the page shows nothing scheduled, exactly as it did for the old `null` (`:31-32`). A 5xx becomes a failure carrying the server's status code instead, so the page can tell "no such event" from "the service is broken" by reading the error rather than by catching an exception.
  - The `CancellationToken` is threaded through the retried GET (`:29`, honoured between attempts as well as by each send), the read (`:33`) and the executor (`:35`), so page disposal cancels an in-flight fetch or a pending backoff and the cancellation propagates rather than being reported as an error.
  - There is no client-side filtering, sorting or time math anywhere in the method: the lists arrive ordered and filtered and are passed straight to the page.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class doc (`:8-13`) states the rationale directly, the endpoint already excludes service, declined and cancelled sessions and does the event-local time math, so nothing is recomputed here. A whole-catalog fetch plus client-side time comparison could not tell an ineligible session apart, since eligibility is not part of a public catalog listing. The call is also cheap to repeat: the server output-caches it under the `NowNextCache` policy (`EventsController.cs:178`) and the payload is identical for every role, so no per-user cache key is needed.
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped as [`INowNextService`](#inownextservice) in the module's UI wiring (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:60`); consumed only by the [`HappeningNow`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page today (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:28`, called at `:156`). `[Rubric §14, Testability]`: covered by three facts in [`NowNextServiceTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), the exact request path (`:20`), the unchanged projection of the server snapshot (`:33`), and the 404-to-`NotFound`-failure degradation (`:50`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the retry lives in the call, not on the named client: no timeout or circuit breaker is configured on this path in the framework registration of `"APIClient"`. A failure that survives the three retries is one transport failure and one localized error message, with a page reload as the next attempt. Note also that the Conference API exposes a second, id-less form of the same snapshot for the home-screen widget (`EventsController.cs:191`); this service does not call it.

### SessionReminderCoordinator

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.HappeningNow` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/HappeningNow/SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:30` · Level 4 · sealed partial class

- **What it is**: the piece that keeps on-device session reminders in step with the user's bookmarks ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2). It owns a tracked session set and two settings in device preferences, and on every change it replans the whole schedule and cancel-then-reschedules the difference (class doc, `:11-18`). The class doc's second paragraph (`:19-25`) adds the one exception to "replan everything": a session already inside the lead window gets its immediate reminder once, when it becomes tracked on this device, and never again on a later replan, unless the replan that should have issued it could not run, in which case the reminder stays owed until one can. The third paragraph (`:26-29`) states the concurrency rule: every read-mutate-write of the tracked set runs one at a time.
- **Depends on**: [`ILocalNotificationService`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#ilocalnotificationservice), [`IDevicePreferences`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#idevicepreferences), [`ISessionLookupService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#isessionlookupservice), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-22-engagement-module.md#iliveeventuiservice), [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner), [`LocalNotificationRequest`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#localnotificationrequest), [`LiveEventContext`](group-22-engagement-module.md#liveeventcontext), [`SessionInfo`](group-22-engagement-module.md#sessioninfo), [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder). Externals: BCL `TimeProvider` (the clock, `:37`), `SemaphoreSlim` (the tracked-set gate, `:55`), `IStringLocalizer<T>`, `ILogger<T>` with a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]`. The class also implements `IDisposable` (`:38`, `:321`) solely to release that gate.
- **Concept introduced, best-effort derived state behind a capability gate.** Two ideas meet here. First, `[Rubric §19, State Management]`: a scheduled local notification is derived state that only stays correct if it tracks the bookmark set, and the bookmark set changes from any page and any device. This class is the one place that derivation lives, and because a resync from a page load can overlap a bookmark toggle, the read-mutate-write cycle is serialized by `_trackedGate` (`:55`): `MutateTrackedAsync` (`:118`) and `ApplySettingsAsync` (`:83`) both `WaitAsync` on it and release in `finally` (`:95-98`, `:139-142`), so neither can overwrite the other's change. Second, `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`: reminders are a convenience and must never fail a bookmark operation, so **every entry point is best-effort**. Both wrap their work in `catch (Exception ex) when (ex is not OperationCanceledException)` and log a warning through `LogReminderSyncFailed` (`:91-94`, `:135-138`, defined `:323-324`), rather than propagating. Note the exception filter: a genuine cancellation is not swallowed. Third, and this is what makes the coupling cheap, every entry point begins with `if (!notifications.IsSupported) return;` (`:78-81`, `:113-116`), before touching the gate, so on a web head the whole class is a no-op that touches no storage and issues no calls. The `[LoggerMessage]` source generator is the framework's structured-logging default, `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: a failure here is invisible to the user by design, so a log entry is the only signal that reminders may be stale.
- **Walkthrough** (constants, then the four entry points, then the private engines)
  - The preference keys and defaults are consts: `EnabledKey` (`:41`) and `LeadMinutesKey` (`:44`) and `DefaultLeadMinutes = 15` (`:52`) are public, while the tracked-set key (`:46`) and the owed-immediate key `OwedImmediateKey` (`:49`) are private. `IsSupported` (`:58`) forwards the capability flag so a settings page can hide the section entirely.
  - `NotifyBookmarkAddedAsync` (`:61-62`) and `NotifyBookmarkRemovedAsync` (`:65-66`) are one-liners over `MutateTrackedAsync`, appending with `Distinct()` and filtering with the default equality comparer for the identifier alias respectively.
  - `ResyncAsync` (`:72-73`) replaces the tracked set outright with an authoritative bookmark map. Its doc (`:68-71`) names the reason it exists: a page that loads the user's bookmarks has the truth in hand, so this is the moment to heal drift caused by changes made on another device.
  - `ApplySettingsAsync` (`:76-99`) persists both settings then replans with an **empty** newly-tracked set (`:89`), which is also how disabling works: the replan cancels everything when `enabled` is false. The empty set means a settings change never newly issues an immediate reminder (it can still issue one that was already owed).
  - `GetSettingsAsync` (`:102-107`) reads the pair back for the device-settings UI, defaulting to enabled and 15 minutes. It takes no gate, since it only reads.
  - `MutateTrackedAsync` (`:109-143`) is the shared write path: take the gate, read the tracked array, apply the caller's mutation, compute `newlyTracked = updated.Except(tracked)` (`:123`), persist the new array (`:124`), cancel reminders for sessions that **left** the set (`:126-131`), then replan the rest passing `newlyTracked` along (`:133`). Cancelling before replanning is deliberate and the comment says so (`:126`). Because `ResyncAsync` runs through here too, a resync on every schedule load only counts the ids that were not already tracked as new.
  - `ReplanAsync(tracked, newlyTracked, ...)` (`:156-168`, doc `:145-155`) is now a thin wrapper that owns the **owed immediate** bookkeeping. It reads the persisted owed ids, keeps only those still tracked, unions in `newlyTracked` (`:161-164`), runs `TryReplanAsync`, and persists the owed set back: emptied when the replan ran, kept as-is when it could not (`:166-167`). That is what stops an immediate reminder from being lost when its one chance to fire hit a denied permission, a missing live event or a failed catalog read.
  - `TryReplanAsync(tracked, owed, ...)` (`:178-239`, doc `:170-177`) is the heart of the class, returns `bool`, and reads as a sequence of early returns, each protecting the existing schedule: reminders switched off, or nothing tracked, cancels everything when off and returns `true`, a completed replan (`:183-193`); a denied OS permission returns `false` without scheduling, and the comment records that the prompt appears at most once and a denial leaves reminders silently off until the user grants them in system settings (`:195-200`); no live event returns `false` (`:202-206`); a catalog read the API refuses returns `false`, with the comment giving the reason, replanning against an empty catalog would cancel reminders that are still valid (`:210-216`). Note the shape of that last guard: `sessionLookup.GetAllAsync` returns a [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) and the code branches on `TryGetValue` (`:213`), so "the API said no" and "there are no sessions" are different outcomes.
  - Past the guards it calls `PlanTrackedSessionsAsync` (`:218-219`), then does the **stale sweep** (`:228-235`): the tracked ids minus the planned ids, cancelled. The comment there (`:221-227`) names the four ways a session can stay tracked yet drop out of the plan (deleted, its event ended, times cleared per BR-16, start moved into the past), notes that nothing upstream cancels those, and explains why the sweep sits **after** a plan was computed rather than beside the early returns: cancelling on a transient null live event would wipe reminders that are still valid. It then hands off to `ScheduleAsync` with the owed set and returns `true` (`:237-238`).
  - `PlanTrackedSessionsAsync` (`:251-286`, doc `:241-250`) no longer narrows the plan to the live event. It builds a map of event id to [`LiveEventContext`](group-22-engagement-module.md#liveeventcontext) seeded with the live event and extended from `liveEventService.GetPublishedEventsAsync` (`:264-268`), drops sessions whose event's live window has closed (`:278`), groups the rest by their own event's IANA zone, falling back to the live event's zone when the event is not in the list (`:279`), and calls [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner)`.Plan` once per zone with the lead time and `timeProvider.GetUtcNow()` (`:280-284`). The effect (A-21 in the doc): a bookmarked session of a later event keeps its reminder while another event is live, and a wrong fallback zone only shifts a reminder until the next replan replaces it by id (`:260-263`).
  - `ScheduleAsync(plan, owed, ...)` (`:291-318`) schedules each planned reminder as a [`LocalNotificationRequest`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#localnotificationrequest), with one skip: a reminder whose `IsImmediate` is set and whose session is not in `owed` is passed over (`:302-305`). The comment (`:298-301`) gives the reasoning: re-issuing it would fire an already-delivered reminder again, and because its id is still in the plan the stale sweep above leaves any still-pending copy alone. The body text does not quote the configured lead: `minutesUntilStart` is the real gap between `StartsAtUtc` and `DeliverAt`, rounded up with a floor of 1 (`:309`), so a clamped reminder does not claim the full lead (`:307-308`). Title and body are localized, the body taking the session title and `minutesUntilStart` as format arguments (`:312-313`), so the wording is resource-driven rather than concatenated. `[Rubric §27, i18n]`.
  - `Dispose` (`:321`) disposes the gate.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) puts device capabilities behind interfaces the web head can answer "unsupported" to, which is what lets this class be registered unconditionally (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:38`, `AddScoped<SessionReminderCoordinator>()`) and injected into a service every head uses. Notification ids are derived deterministically from the session id ([`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner)`.GetNotificationId`, `SessionReminderPlanner.cs:47-48`), which is what makes rescheduling **replace** instead of duplicate, and is why a replan-everything strategy is affordable. The two things a blind replan would get wrong are both handled by the owed set: re-firing a clamped immediate reminder on every resync (prevented because only owed ids pass the `ScheduleAsync` skip), and silently losing one whose replan could not run (prevented because `ReplanAsync` keeps the owed ids when `TryReplanAsync` answers `false`). Reading the clock through an injected `TimeProvider` (`:37`, used at `:258`) instead of `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` puts the coordinator under the same fixed-clock testability as the planner; the Engagement UI wiring registers `TimeProvider.System` with `TryAddSingleton` because no UI head otherwise does (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:70-72`).
- **Where it's used**: injected as a concrete class by [`SessionBookmarkUIService`](#sessionbookmarkuiservice) (`SessionBookmarkUIService.cs:28`), which drives all three notify or resync members, and by the host's device-settings page (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI/Pages/DeviceSettings.razor:7`), which reads the settings (`:70`), writes them on change (`:92`, `:98`) and seeds its lead-time control from `DefaultLeadMinutes` (`:64`). `[Rubric §14, Testability]`: [`SessionReminderCoordinatorTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) holds seventeen `[Fact]`/`[Theory]` methods covering the branches above (stale-sweep cases, no live event, an unsupported host, lookup failures never throwing out of the coordinator, a failed catalog read leaving the schedule alone, a resync not re-issuing an immediate reminder, and an immediate reminder naming the real remaining time).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `IsSupported` (`:58`) has no consumer in the repo today, including the device-settings page its doc points at; the page relies on the internal no-ops instead. The class is registered **scoped** while the state it manages is device-global, so the `_trackedGate` serializes only calls made through one instance: correctness across instances rests on preferences being the single source of truth and on every mutation replanning from scratch. Whether a scheduled notification actually fires is the platform's business, outside this file.

### EventFeedback

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/EventFeedback.razor.cs:19` · Level 4 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the attendee event-feedback page (`@page "/feedback/event/{EventId:int}"`, `EventFeedback.razor:1`). It loads the event name and its dynamic question set, pre-fills any answers the attendee already submitted, and posts the answered questions back as a single atomic upsert (BR-107, per the class doc comment at `EventFeedback.razor.cs:15-18`). A refused submit reports the server's own stated message and lists its field errors in the page's `ErrorSummary`, while an unexpected fault keeps a generic toast. A failed load offers a Retry, while an event that does not exist does not. The route id arrives already typed (`[Parameter] EventIdentifierType EventId`, `:27`) and a change of that id reloads the form in place.
- **Depends on**: [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice), [`IEventFeedbackUIService`](#ieventfeedbackuiservice), [`IEventLookupService`](group-21-conference-ui.md#ieventlookupservice), [`IToastService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itoastservice), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`EventQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswerdto), [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel), [`ModelValidation`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#modelvalidation), [`DataAnnotationsModelValidator`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#dataannotationsmodelvalidator), [`LatestLoadGuard`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#latestloadguard) (the superseding-load guard, `:42`) and [`DetailLoadState`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#detailloadstate) (through the markup's `DetailLoadStateContent`, `EventFeedback.razor:26`). Externals: Blazor `NavigationManager`, MudBlazor `MudForm` / `BreadcrumbItem`, `System.Globalization`, and from the markup an injected `IStringLocalizer<EventFeedback>` `L` (`EventFeedback.razor:7`) plus the shared `UnsavedChangesGuard` (`EventFeedback.razor:11`) and `ErrorSummary` (`EventFeedback.razor:104`) components. The failure path also uses the `Result` type's `HasErrorType` / `NotifyOnFailure` helpers and `ErrorType.Unexpected` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:313`, `:320`), and the submit and delete awaits take `LifetimeToken`.
- **Concept**: the dynamic feedback form (the `_answers` map, the single validation delegate, the `_missingRequired` required-answer gate, the `UnsavedChangesGuard` pairing and the `StateHasChanged`-before-navigate ordering) is taught on [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback) and is the same shape here, with one difference: the session page has moved its question, answer and saved-id state into [`SessionFeedbackAnswers`](#sessionfeedbackanswers), whereas this page still holds that state inline (`_questions` `:67`, `_answers` `:68`, `_missingRequired` `:75`, `_existingAnswerIds` `:76`). The submit strategy matches the session page: `SubmitFeedbackAsync` calls `FeedbackService.SubmitAnswersAsync` once with the whole answered set (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:281`), so the server applies the batch atomically and there is nothing partial left to report. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` still applies: an unanswered required question, rating included, is caught locally before any request (`:258-262`), an empty submit on an all-optional form is a local no-op rather than a request the batch endpoint would refuse as empty (`:277-279`), and a failed submit leaves `_isDirty` set so the same Submit button retries. Two things carry over from the session page. First, the submit and delete awaits read `LifetimeToken` (`:34`, `_cts.LifetimeToken()`) instead of `_cts.Token`: reading `Token` off a disposed source throws `ObjectDisposedException`, whereas `Dispose` cancels before it disposes, so a handler resuming after navigation gets a cancelled token and exits through its `OperationCanceledException` path. Second, a refused submit goes through `ReportSubmitFailure` (`:311-321`): an `ErrorType.Unexpected` result (a 500, transport failure or timeout) keeps the generic `Snackbar.SubmitFailed` toast (`:313-317`), while any other refusal is stored in `_submitResult` (`:91`, `:319`) so `ErrorSummary` lists its field errors after the snackbar times out, and `NotifyOnFailure` toasts the server's own message (`:320`; ADR-027 Decision 9 carve-out per the method's doc comment). `_submitResult` is cleared at the start of each submit (`:265`).
- **Walkthrough of what differs from [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback)**
  - There is no precondition gate and no `_preconditionMessage`: an event either loads or it does not. `OnInitialized` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:96-108`) builds both validation delegates, `_validateAnswerText` and `_validateAnswerEmail`, from one prototype [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel) and one `DataAnnotationsModelValidator(L)` (`:98-101`), and also builds the breadcrumb trail (`:102-107`).
  - Loading is parameter-driven. `OnParametersSetAsync` (`:110-119`) compares `EventId` with `_loadedEventId` (`:41`); a feedback deep link to another event while this form is open reuses the routed instance with only a new `EventId`, so a new id starts `LoadFormAsync` (`:121`) and an unchanged id does nothing.
  - `LoadFormAsync` (`:121-197`) first calls `_load.Begin()` (`:124`), which supersedes and cancels the previous event's load, then resets everything that belonged to the previous form, `_loadNotFound` included (`:127-136`). It resolves the event name from `EventLookup.GetAllAsync(token)` (`:141`), setting a localized `_loadError` when the lookup itself fails (`:145`); when the id is absent from it, it also sets `_loadNotFound` (`:155-156`). That flag (`:65`) picks the markup's `DetailLoadStateContent` state (`EventFeedback.razor:25-29`): `NotFound` for an event that does not exist, otherwise `Failed` with a Retry wired to `LoadFormAsync`. Each await is followed by `token.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` (`:142`, `:162`, `:176`), so a superseded load stops rather than writing stale state. The rest (questions ordered by `Sort` at `:169`, one [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel) per question at `:172`, the pre-fill at `:174-183`) matches the session page step for step. A cancelled load is swallowed (`:185-188`), and `IsLoading` is cleared only when `_load.IsCurrent(generation)` still holds (`:189-196`), so a superseded load leaves the flag to the load that replaced it.
  - `PreFillExistingAnswers` (`:199-227`) applies the same cross-entity guard, skipping answers whose `EventId` does not match the current event (`:205`).
  - `FlagMissingRequiredAnswers` (`:234-244`) and its `_missingRequired` set (`:75`, bound to each question's field at `EventFeedback.razor:52`) are the session page's logic, kept inline.
  - `SubmitFeedbackAsync` (`:246`) validates the form, then flags unanswered required questions and returns with a `Snackbar.RequiredFields` warning if any (`:253`, `:260`), then projects the answered questions into `pending`, dropping blanks (`:271-275`). If `pending` is non-empty it posts the whole set in one call to `SubmitAnswersAsync(EventId, ...)` (`:279-281`); on failure it calls `ReportSubmitFailure` and returns with `_isDirty` still set (`:282-286`), otherwise it clears `_isDirty` and toasts `Snackbar.FeedbackSubmitted` with `pending.Count` (`:289-290`). The id is the typed parameter, so there is no parse step.
  - `NavigateBack` (`:356-357`) returns to the Conference event detail route, formatting the id with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`.
  - `DeleteAnswerAsync` (`:323-354`), `GetAnswerValue` (`:384-394`) and the disposable pattern (`:359-382`, which also disposes `_load` at `:372`) are the session page's, unchanged in mechanism. In particular a cleared answer leaves `_isDirty` as it is (`:346-347`): the delete commits only that answer on the server, so unsaved edits to other answers keep the unsaved-changes guard armed.
- **Why it's built this way**: the page owns orchestration only, all I/O goes through injected UI service interfaces, which keeps it testable under bUnit and swappable per host. Moving to a single batch `SubmitAnswersAsync` call removes the partial-save case entirely: a refusal now means nothing was written, so the retry story is a plain resend rather than a per-question accounting. Reloading from `OnParametersSetAsync` behind a `LatestLoadGuard` means a route change to a different event cannot leave the previous event's questions or answers on screen, or let a slow earlier load overwrite a newer one. Sharing the rest of the form shape with [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback) by convention rather than through a base class keeps each page self-contained and independently editable.
- **Where it's used**: rendered at `/feedback/event/{EventId:int}`, reached from the public event detail page's `SubmitFeedback` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Public/Events/PublicEventDetail.razor.cs:193`). The same route is also expressed as [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.EventFeedback(eventId)` (`EngagementRoutePaths.cs:17`). Its dependencies are registered by the Engagement UI [`DependencyInjection`](#dependencyinjection-3). Component-tested by [`EventFeedbackTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the markup, the resource strings and `UnsavedChangesGuard`'s own behavior live outside this file; this section covers the code-behind plus the bindings its members exist to serve.

### SessionFeedback

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/SessionFeedback.razor.cs:16` · Level 6 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the attendee session-feedback page (`@page "/feedback/session/{SessionId:int}"`, `SessionFeedback.razor:1`). It checks three business preconditions before it will render the question form at all, then loads the dynamic question set, pre-fills any answers the attendee already submitted, and posts the whole form back in one atomic call. The question, answer and saved-id state sits in a [`SessionFeedbackAnswers`](#sessionfeedbackanswers) instance (`:65`), so this class holds the lifecycle, the preconditions and the I/O.
- **Depends on**: [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice), [`ISessionFeedbackUIService`](#isessionfeedbackuiservice), [`IEntityService<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#ientityservicetentitydto-tidentifiertype) (bound as `IEntityService<SessionDTO, SessionIdentifierType>`, `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:20`), [`IToastService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itoastservice), [`SessionFeedbackAnswers`](#sessionfeedbackanswers), [`LatestLoadGuard`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#latestloadguard), [`DetailLoadState`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#detailloadstate) (through the markup's `DetailLoadStateContent`, `SessionFeedback.razor:26`), [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel), [`ModelValidation`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#modelvalidation) and [`DataAnnotationsModelValidator`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#dataannotationsmodelvalidator), [`SessionStatuses`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionstatuses)`.IsEligible` for the BR-49 check, plus the `IsNotFound()` result extension from `MMCA.Common.UI.Common`. Externals: Blazor `NavigationManager`, MudBlazor `MudForm` / `BreadcrumbItem`, and from the markup an injected `IStringLocalizer<SessionFeedback>` `L` (`SessionFeedback.razor:7`), the shared `UnsavedChangesGuard` component (`SessionFeedback.razor:11`) and `ErrorSummary` (`SessionFeedback.razor:115`). The failure path also uses the `Result` helpers `HasErrorType` / `NotifyOnFailure` with `ErrorType.Unexpected` (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:274`, `:281`).
- **Concept introduced, the precondition-gated dynamic form.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses component/logic separation: the markup lives in `SessionFeedback.razor`, the behavior in this `partial class`, and the form's answer state in [`SessionFeedbackAnswers`](#sessionfeedbackanswers), so the page's flow is reviewable apart from its layout and its state rules. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` is the load-bearing category here and it shows up four times. First, `CheckPreconditions` (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:187`) refuses *before* rendering inputs, so when feedback is not permitted the attendee gets one sentence explaining why rather than a form that fails on submit. Second, validation is declarative and single-sourced: `_validateAnswerText` (`:73`) and `_validateAnswerEmail` (`:77`) are two delegates built from [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel)'s annotations (`TextValue` and `EmailValue`) and handed to the text and email fields respectively, so the 4000-character rule is never written twice and the email-format rule lives on the model rather than in the page. Third, a required check covers what `MudForm` cannot: `MudRating` is not a form input, so form validation never blocks an unanswered required rating, and the missing-required set on the answer state (`SessionFeedbackAnswers.MissingRequired`, filled by `FlagMissingRequiredAnswers`, called at `:223`) does, for every question type, and drives the inline error on each question's field (`SessionFeedback.razor:63`). Fourth, `UnsavedChangesGuard IsDirty="_isDirty"` wraps the page, so navigating away with unsaved edits prompts first. The dynamic part is the interesting one: the questions are data, not markup, so the render is a `switch` over `question.QuestionType?.ToUpperInvariant()` (`SessionFeedback.razor:65`) that picks a `MudRating` (`case "RATING"`, `:67`), an email `MudTextField` bound to `EmailValue` and validated by `_validateAnswerEmail` (`case "EMAIL"`, `:72-77`), or a three-line multiline `MudTextField`, and each field's required flag and `RequiredError` come from the DTO rather than from the page. `[Rubric §19, State Management]` covers the answer state; `[Rubric §27, i18n]` covers the fact that every visible string, error messages included, is an `L["..."]` lookup.
- **Walkthrough** (teaching order)
  - Injected services (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:18-22`) and the `[Parameter] SessionIdentifierType SessionId` (`:24`) arrive from the route. The parameter is the typed id alias, and the route template carries the `:int` constraint (`SessionFeedback.razor:1`), so the router parses it and the page needs no parse step.
  - `LifetimeToken` (`:31`, `_cts.LifetimeToken()`) is the token the submit and delete awaits use. Reading `Token` off a disposed `CancellationTokenSource` throws `ObjectDisposedException`; `Dispose` cancels before it disposes, so a handler resuming after the attendee navigated away gets a cancelled token and stops through its `OperationCanceledException` path (`:28-30`).
  - `IsLoading` / `IsSubmitting` (`:41-42`) and `SubmitButtonText` (`:44-55`) drive the chrome: the button reads "Submitting" while a post is in flight, then "Update" or "Submit" depending on `_answerState.HasExistingFeedback` (`:53`). The private state fields hold the load error (`:57`), the `_loadNotFound` flag that says the error reports a session that does not exist rather than a failed load (`:59-60`), the precondition message (`:61`), the session title (`:62`), the `_answerState` (`:65`), the `MudForm` reference (`:66`), the `_isDirty` flag (`:67`), the two validation delegates (`:73`, `:77`) and `_submitResult` (`:80`), the last submit's refusal kept so `ErrorSummary` can list its field errors. `MarkDirty` (`:82`) is the one-line handler every input's `@bind-...:after` calls.
  - `OnInitialized` (`:85-97`) builds both delegates once, by handing a throwaway [`FeedbackAnswerModel`](#feedbackanswermodel) prototype, the `model => model.TextValue` and `model => model.EmailValue` selectors and one `DataAnnotationsModelValidator(L)` to `ModelValidation.ForProperty` (`:87-90`), and builds the three-item breadcrumb trail (`:91-96`). The strongly-typed `ForProperty` overload (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Validation/ModelValidation.cs:69`) is the right one here because these fields have no `For` expression and the value being validated has not been written to a model yet; the localizer resolves the `ErrorMessage` resource key ([ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html)).
  - Loading is parameter-driven. `OnParametersSetAsync` (`:99-108`) compares `SessionId` with `_loadedSessionId` (`:38`); a feedback deep link to another session while this form is open reuses the routed instance with only a new `SessionId`, so a new id starts `LoadFormAsync` (`:110`) and an unchanged id does nothing. `LoadFormAsync` begins with `_load.Begin()` (`:113`), which supersedes and cancels the previous session's load, and resets everything that belonged to the previous form, including `_loadNotFound = false` and `_answerState = new()` (`:116-123`).
  - It then loads the session through `SessionService.GetByIdAsync(SessionId, false, token)` (`:128`). The failure branch (`:130-139`) splits the two reasons apart: `_loadNotFound = sessionResult.IsNotFound()` (`:134`) picks the page's own "session not found" wording for a genuine 404 and the generic load failure for anything else (`:135-137`). The same flag drives the markup's `DetailLoadStateContent` (`SessionFeedback.razor:25-29`), which offers a Retry wired to `LoadFormAsync` for a failed load and none for a session that does not exist.
  - `CheckPreconditions(session)` (`:187`) runs next (call at `:143`), and a returned message stops the load **before** any questions are fetched (`:144-148`). Only then does it load the `"Session"` questions (`:151`), hand them to `_answerState.SetQuestions` (`:160`), and pre-fill from the server with `_answerState.PreFill(SessionId, existingAnswers)` (`:162-171`). Each await is followed by `token.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` (`:129`, `:152`, `:164`); `OperationCanceledException` is swallowed as expected on disposal or supersession (`:173-176`), and in `finally` `IsLoading` is cleared only when `_load.IsCurrent(generation)` still holds (`:177-184`), so a superseded load leaves the flag to the load that replaced it.
  - `CheckPreconditions` (`:187-209`) encodes three rules, each returning a localized message. BR-91 rejects service sessions (`session.IsServiceSession`, `:190`). BR-49 rejects any session [`SessionStatuses`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionstatuses)`.IsEligible` does not allow, an unset `Status` or `"Accepted"` (case-insensitive) are the only eligible values (`:196`), the same shared allow-list Conference applies elsewhere. BR-16 rejects unscheduled sessions, where `StartsAt` or `EndsAt` is null (`:203`), because session feedback is a time-gated action. Returning `null` means feedback is allowed.
  - Pre-fill and the required-answer gate are no longer page methods. `SessionFeedbackAnswers.PreFill` (`SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:58-86`) keeps the cross-session defense in depth and records each saved answer id, which is what enables the per-question clear button; `FlagMissingRequiredAnswers` (`SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:93-103`) clears the missing set and adds the id of every required question whose value is blank, for any type.
  - `SubmitFeedbackAsync` (`:211`) validates the form and toasts a warning if it is invalid (`:218`), then runs `_answerState.FlagMissingRequiredAnswers()` and toasts the same `Snackbar.RequiredFields` warning and returns when it reports a gap (`:223-227`), so an unanswered required rating, or an entirely empty submit on a form with a required question, never reaches the server. It then takes the answered questions as a `pending` list of `(QuestionId, AnswerValue)` tuples from `_answerState.PendingAnswers()` (`:236`), which drops blanks. An empty list (possible only when every question is optional) short-circuits: with nothing answered there is no form to send, so the submit stays a local no-op rather than a request the batch endpoint would refuse (`:238-240`). Otherwise it makes **one** call, `FeedbackService.SubmitAnswersAsync(SessionId, pending, LifetimeToken)` (`:242`), which the interface documents as applied atomically server-side (`IFeedbackUIService.cs:66-73`). `_submitResult` is reset to `null` first (`:230`). A failure therefore wrote nothing: the page calls `ReportSubmitFailure(result)` (`:245`, defined `:272-282`) and returns with `_isDirty` still set, so the same Submit button retries. That method keeps the generic `Snackbar.SubmitFailed` toast for an `ErrorType.Unexpected` result (a 500, transport failure or timeout, `:274-278`, the ADR-027 Decision 9 carve-out), and otherwise stores the result in `_submitResult` (`:280`) so `ErrorSummary` (`SessionFeedback.razor:115`) lists the field errors after the snackbar times out, and `NotifyOnFailure` toasts the server's own stated message (`:281`). On success it clears `_isDirty` (`:250`), toasts the count (`:251`), then calls `StateHasChanged()` **before** `NavigateBack()` (`:253-254`); the inline comment (`:252`) explains why: the flush lets `UnsavedChangesGuard`'s `IsDirty` parameter update before `NavigateTo` fires the `LocationChanging` handler, otherwise the guard would prompt over changes that were just saved.
  - `DeleteAnswerAsync` (`:284`) clears one previously-saved answer: it looks the answer id up with `_answerState.TryGetSavedAnswerId` (`:286`), calls `FeedbackService.DeleteAnswerAsync(SessionId, answerId, LifetimeToken)` (`:293`), then `_answerState.ClearSavedAnswer(questionId)` (`:300`), which removes the id and resets the model (`TextValue` and `RatingValue`; `EmailValue` follows `TextValue`). It leaves `_isDirty` as it is (`:302-303`): the delete commits only that answer on the server, so unsaved edits to other answers keep the unsaved-changes guard armed.
  - `NavigateBack` (`:312-313`) returns to the Conference session detail route through `EngagementRoutePaths.SessionDetails(SessionId)`. The static bridge from a `FeedbackAnswerModel` back to a wire string is now `SessionFeedbackAnswers.GetAnswerValue` (`SessionFeedbackAnswers.cs:125-135`), returning `TextValue` for text and email types and treating `RatingValue > 0` as the only answered rating.
  - `Dispose(bool)` / `Dispose()` (`:317-338`) implement the standard disposable pattern, cancelling and disposing `_cts` and disposing `_load` (`:326-328`) so in-flight loads stop when the attendee leaves.
- **Why it's built this way**: pushing the three business rules into one `CheckPreconditions` method keeps the render path binary (either a precondition message or the form) and makes each BR individually reviewable and individually testable. Moving the answer state into [`SessionFeedbackAnswers`](#sessionfeedbackanswers) means the page replaces one object to discard a stale form and the pre-fill, required-gate and projection rules can be read without the lifecycle code around them. The single batch submit is the more consequential choice, and [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) makes the same one: because the server applies a whole session form atomically, there is no partially-saved state to report to the attendee, so the failure path is a plain retry rather than a "3 of 5 saved" message. The cancellation-token discipline (the disposal-safe `LifetimeToken` on submit and delete, the superseding `LatestLoadGuard` token on load), the split between a stated refusal and an unexpected fault in `ReportSubmitFailure`, and the `StateHasChanged`-before-navigate ordering are correctness, not style.
- **Where it's used**: rendered at `/feedback/session/{SessionId:int}`, reached from the public session detail page's `SubmitFeedback` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Public/Sessions/PublicSessionDetail.razor.cs:316`). The same route is also expressed as [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.SessionFeedback(sessionId)` (`EngagementRoutePaths.cs:18`). Its dependencies are registered by the Engagement UI [`DependencyInjection`](#dependencyinjection-3). Component-tested by [`SessionFeedbackTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the markup, the resource strings and `UnsavedChangesGuard`'s own behavior live outside this file. The BR numbers in the comments are the page's own claim about which business rule it implements; the authoritative statements live in the ADC specifications guide, not in this file.

### AttendeeRow

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/AttendeeLookupService.cs:220` · Level 0 · record (private sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: the wire shape of one row returned by the generic paged Identity users endpoint (`users/paged`, `AttendeeLookupService.cs:25`), declared privately inside [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice) purely so `System.Text.Json` has something to bind to. It never leaves the service: every row is projected into the public [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) before it reaches a caller.
- **Depends on**: the `UserIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: `System.Text.Json` binding at the call site, wrapped in [`PagedCollectionResult<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt).
- **Concept introduced, the private deserialization shape.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses how a consumer expresses the contract it reads. Two types for one payload looks redundant until you read what the private one is allowed to be: the doc comment (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:215-219`) records that this is the Identity roster row, whose identifier is `id`, that only the fields the check-in surfaces render are read, and that the lock state and live session count the row also carries are ignored, which is exactly the tolerant-reader posture. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` is the other half: Engagement redeclares five fields instead of referencing the Identity assembly for its list DTO (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:6-9` states that constraint for the public twin), so Identity can move, be versioned, or add columns without a compile-time edit here.
- **Walkthrough** (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:220-225`): five positional members, `Id`, `Email`, `FirstName`, `LastName`, `Role`, structurally identical to [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) minus its computed `DisplayName` (the identifier is `Id` here, matching the generic paged row, where the public twin calls it `UserId`). It is bound as `PagedCollectionResult<AttendeeRow>` (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:71-72`) so one read picks up both the page items and the pagination metadata, and converted one row at a time by the private `ToSummary` (`:213-214`), which maps `row.Id` onto `UserId`.
- **Why it's built this way**: `private sealed` keeps the wire shape from becoming an accidental public contract. The projection point is the single place where a server rename would have to be absorbed, and the computed display fallback lives on the public record rather than on the row, so the transport type stays inert data.
- **Where it's used**: only inside [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice), by `SearchAsync` (`:54`, `:72-79`) and, through it, by the roster snapshot `LoadRosterAsync` builds (`:132-165`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing at compile time keeps this shape aligned with what Identity actually serializes. A renamed server property binds to the member default rather than failing the build; only [AttendeeLookupServiceTests](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup) round-tripping a JSON body catches it.

### AttendeeSearchField

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/IAttendeeLookupService.cs:41` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: which one of last name, first name or email the organizer's single search box applies to when looking an attendee up at the door.
- **Depends on**: nothing (a bare `enum`).
- **Concept introduced, the enum that exists because of a server semantic.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a client contract makes the server's actual behavior usable. The type doc states the reason outright (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:36-40`): the Identity users endpoint **ANDs** its filters, so broadcasting one typed term across all three fields would match nobody. A UI that offered one box and no field picker would therefore be a UI that silently never finds anyone. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` covers the resulting shape: one box plus one explicit selector, so the organizer always knows which field the term is being matched against.
- **Walkthrough** (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:41-51`): three members with explicit values, `LastName = 0` (`:44`), `FirstName = 1` (`:47`), `Email = 2` (`:50`). The explicit ordinals matter because the value is round-tripped as a string through the grid's filter state and parsed back with `Enum.TryParse` ([`AttendeeSearchPanel`](#attendeesearchpanel)`.razor.cs:49-51`), which falls back to `LastName` when the stored value does not parse. `LastName` is also the field's initial value (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:36`), which is the field an organizer reads off a badge first.
- **Why it's built this way**: an enum rather than a raw string keeps the three-way mapping exhaustive and reviewable in one `switch` (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:116-122`), which projects the selected member onto the matching [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) property name and writes a single `("contains", term)` filter (`:120`).
- **Where it's used**: only by [`AttendeeSearchPanel`](#attendeesearchpanel), as the bound value of a `MudSelect` (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor:15-22`) and as the discriminator in its filter builder; the resulting filter is what [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice)`.SearchAsync` receives as its `email` / `firstName` / `lastName` arguments (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:73-79`).

### SessionInfo

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/ISessionLookupService.cs:9` · Level 0 · record

- **What it is**: the lightweight projection of a Conference session carrying just what Engagement pages need to label a bookmark, a check-in row or a live row: id, title, optional start and end times, and the owning event id. It sits at the top of `ISessionLookupService.cs` because it is the shape that lookup contract returns.
- **Depends on**: the `SessionIdentifierType` and `EventIdentifierType` aliases (Conference identifier aliases, solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). No first-party service dependencies.
- **Concept introduced, the cross-module display projection.** Engagement owns no session table (database-per-service, [ADR-006](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/006-database-per-service.html)), so it cannot join to a session title: it fetches the fields it needs over HTTP and holds them in this small record, a deliberately thinner shape than Conference's own [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto). `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses how a consumer models another service's data at its boundary; declaring five fields here means a change to unrelated `SessionDTO` members never ripples into Engagement. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` is the same decision seen from the extraction side, and it is the identical posture the neighbouring [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) takes against Identity: the consumer redeclares the view it renders rather than depending on the producer's assembly.
- **Walkthrough** (`ISessionLookupService.cs:9-14`): five positional members, `(SessionIdentifierType Id, string Title, DateTime? StartsAt, DateTime? EndsAt, EventIdentifierType EventId)`. `StartsAt` and `EndsAt` are nullable because a session imported from the schedule source may not yet have times assigned, so every consumer has to tolerate an unscheduled session. It is a plain `public record`, not `sealed`.
- **Why it's built this way**: a compact record lets [`SessionLookupService`](#sessionlookupservice) build a whole-catalog dictionary cheaply, and value equality plus `init`-only members mean a page can hold, compare and sort the rows client-side without a second fetch per row.
- **Where it's used**: returned by [`ISessionLookupService`](#isessionlookupservice) (a catalog keyed by id, or one row), constructed by [`SessionLookupService`](#sessionlookupservice) from [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto) payloads (`SessionLookupService.cs:72-73`, `:64-65`); consumed by [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) as its session list (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:55`), by [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner) as the bookmarked-session input it schedules against (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:58`), and by the live-session pages (`Pages/SessionLive/SessionLive.razor.cs`, `Pages/SessionLive/PresenterView.razor.cs`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing here constrains `Title` to non-empty or the times to a sane order; both come straight off the Conference payload, and the nullable times are the only shape signal that an unscheduled session exists.

### ISessionLookupService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/ISessionLookupService.cs:19` · Level 3 · interface

- **What it is**: the contract for fetching Conference session data for display enrichment inside Engagement pages, returning [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo) projections. Two members: a whole-catalog read and a single-session read, both `Result`-typed.
- **Depends on**: [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) (`MMCA.Common.Shared.Abstractions`, imported at `ISessionLookupService.cs:1`), the `SessionIdentifierType` alias. Externals: BCL `IReadOnlyDictionary` and `Task`.
- **Concept introduced, two reads with an explicit efficiency contract, and absence expressed as a typed failure.** The doc on `GetAllAsync` (`ISessionLookupService.cs:21-26`) is unusually prescriptive: use it "only when a page genuinely needs the whole set (e.g. the bookmark reminder planner, which schedules against every bookmarked session)", while single-session pages "must use `GetByIdAsync` instead of transferring the catalog to label one row". That is the interface teaching its own performance discipline. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether whole-collection transfers are avoided on paths that render one item; the split-method contract encodes the fast path in the type rather than in a review comment. The second idea is the `Result` posture: `GetByIdAsync`'s doc states that a missing session answers an `ErrorType.NotFound` failure rather than the old `null` (`:31-32`), so callers branch on an [`ErrorType`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#errortype) instead of on a null check that could not distinguish "no such session" from "the fetch broke".
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAllAsync(CancellationToken)` (`:27-28`) returns `Result<IReadOnlyDictionary<SessionIdentifierType, SessionInfo>>`, the catalog keyed by id, so a page can label many rows with O(1) lookups.
  - `GetByIdAsync(SessionIdentifierType, CancellationToken)` (`:34-36`) returns `Result<SessionInfo>` and fails with `NotFound` rather than answering an empty value.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping both shapes on one interface lets a page pick the cost that matches its need while the implementation shares one HTTP client and one mapping; typing both on [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) means a Conference outage is a renderable state, not an unhandled exception on a bookmark list. Note the contrast with the sibling [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice), which drops `Result` on its naming member on purpose: here both reads feed content a page actually renders, so both failures matter.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`SessionLookupService`](#sessionlookupservice), registered scoped (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:54`). Injected by [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:28`), [`OrganizerAttendance`](#organizerattendance), the live-session pages, and [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator), which is the whole-catalog case the `GetAllAsync` doc names.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the "must use `GetByIdAsync`" rule is a doc comment, not a compiler or test constraint; nothing fails the build if a single-session page calls `GetAllAsync`.

### QuestionLookupService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/QuestionLookupService.cs:19` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the HTTP implementation of [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice). It fetches the user-authored feedback questions for one entity type ("Event" or "Session") from the Conference-owned `questions` resource.
- **Depends on**: [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice), [`AuthenticatedServiceBase`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#authenticatedservicebase), [`ITokenStorageService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#itokenstorageservice), [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor), [`PagedReadAll`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pagedreadall), [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`PagedCollectionResult<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt). Externals: `IHttpClientFactory`.
- **Concept**: the same authenticated-HTTP shape as [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice), used here to reach a **Conference**-owned read endpoint from the Engagement UI. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether a module reaches across a boundary through a contract that survives extraction: this one already does, because the call is plain HTTP through the Gateway rather than a project reference into Conference. The Engagement UI references only [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto) from `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared` (`QuestionLookupService.cs:2`), which is the wire contract, not the implementation.
- **Walkthrough** (`:27-53`): `GetQuestionsAsync(questionEntity, ct)` escapes the caller-supplied value once (`:31`), then hands a per-page fetch to [`PagedReadAll`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pagedreadall)`.ReadAllAsync` (`:33-50`). Each page URL (`:39-40`) constrains `QuestionEntity` to the escaped argument and `QuestionSource` to `User`, carries `pageNumber`, `pageSize=100` (the `PageSize` constant, `:24`) and `sortColumn=Sort&sortDirection=asc` (built from `nameof(QuestionDTO.Sort)`). The `Uri.EscapeDataString` on the caller-supplied value is the one input-handling detail worth naming: the argument is a `string`, not an enum, so escaping it is what keeps a stray character from reshaping the query. The retry is the token-aware overload (`:42-44`) and each envelope is read as a [`PagedCollectionResult<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt) (`:46-47`). `ReadAllAsync` keeps requesting pages until the reported total is reached, and the accumulated list is `Map`ped to an `IReadOnlyList` (`:52`), so a failure travels on untouched while a success arrives already unwrapped.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class doc's remarks record the reason for paging: a single page read stopped silently at the questions API's 100-row clamp, and in API order, so a form with more questions than that lost the rest; sorting by `Sort` also keeps the form in its authored order, and a result that fits one page is still exactly one request. Filtering `QuestionSource` to `User` server-side rather than fetching everything and filtering in the page keeps system-authored questions off the wire entirely, and pushing `QuestionEntity` into the query lets the same service serve both feedback pages with no branching.
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped as [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice) (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:41`); injected by both feedback pages, [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:21`) and [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback) (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:18`). Covered by [`QuestionLookupServiceTests`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `questionEntity` is an unconstrained `string` in both the interface (`IFeedbackUIService.cs:15`) and this implementation, so the valid values ("Event", "Session") are a convention the callers hold, not a type the compiler checks. The `pageSize=100` is the per-request size, not a ceiling on the result: paging continues until the reported total, bounded only by `PagedReadAll`'s own page cap (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/PagedReadAll.cs:27`, 40 pages).

### SessionLookupService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services.Lookups` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/Lookups/SessionLookupService.cs:13` · Level 4 · sealed class

- **What it is**: the HTTP implementation of [`ISessionLookupService`](#isessionlookupservice). It fetches sessions from the Conference-owned `sessions` resource and builds either a session-keyed lookup of [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo) projections or one row.
- **Depends on**: [`ISessionLookupService`](#isessionlookupservice), [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo), [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto) (Conference shared), [`PagedCollectionResult<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt), [`PagedReadAll`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pagedreadall), [`HttpResultExecutor`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#httpresultexecutor), [`ProblemDetailsResultReader`](group-08-auth.md#problemdetailsresultreader), [`IdempotentReadRetry`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#idempotentreadretry). Externals: `IHttpClientFactory` (`SessionLookupService.cs:13`), `System.Globalization`. Note what is **absent**: unlike its two neighbours in this folder, it does not derive from [`AuthenticatedServiceBase`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#authenticatedservicebase) and takes no token storage, because it reads the plain `"APIClient"` (`:29`, `:51`) against a public, output-cached listing. It still gets the base class's retry, through the static [`IdempotentReadRetry`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#idempotentreadretry) rather than through inheritance.
- **Concept introduced, the paged catalog transfer that stops at the reported total.** `GetAllAsync` hands a one-page fetch to [`PagedReadAll`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pagedreadall)`.ReadAllAsync` (`:25-39`), which requests pages 1, 2, ... until a page is empty or the accumulated count reaches the total the server reports, giving up after 40 pages (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/PagedReadAll.cs:27`, `:57-89`). The inline comment (`:20-24`) records why a single read was replaced: the base `/sessions` endpoint serves one page capped at `MaxPageSize` (500) of the catalog of every edition, sorted by `StartsAt`, so once the catalog passed 500 the current event's sessions silently fell off the end (the check-in picker, the attendance titles and the session reminders all lost them). Each page URL comes from `PagedReadAll.LookupPageUrl("sessions", pageNumber)` (`:32`), which sorts by `Id` so consecutive pages neither skip nor repeat a row and sets `includeFKs=false&includeChildren=false` (`PagedReadAll.cs:42-45`). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses transfer sizing: the trimmed query shrinks each page on the wire and the five-field [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo) projection shrinks it on the heap, so the whole-catalog read the interface sanctions stays cheap while no longer truncating. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]`: the class consumes Conference through the wire contract only, referencing [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto) and nothing else from the other module.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `GetAllAsync` (`:17-42`) passes `PagedReadAll.ReadAllAsync` a per-page delegate (`:26-38`) that runs inside `HttpResultExecutor.ExecuteAsync` (`:26-27`), sends the GET through `IdempotentReadRetry.GetAsync(httpClient, ...)` (`:30-33`) and reads the response as a `PagedCollectionResult<SessionDTO>` through `ProblemDetailsResultReader.ReadAsync` (`:35-36`). The accumulated `Result<List<SessionDTO>>` then goes through `sessions.Map(BuildLookup)` (`:41`). `BuildLookup` (`:67-77`) fills a `Dictionary<SessionIdentifierType, SessionInfo>` by looping the list and returns it as `IReadOnlyDictionary`. Because `Map` runs on success only, no null guard is needed: a failed page short-circuits `ReadAllAsync`, which returns that first failure unchanged.
  - `GetByIdAsync` (`:44-65`) is an expression-bodied method that returns the `Task` from `HttpResultExecutor.ExecuteAsync` directly (`:45-47`, no `async`/`await` of its own, since the only awaits live inside the delegate). It GETs `sessions/{sessionId}` through the same `IdempotentReadRetry.GetAsync` (`:53-56`) with an invariant-culture interpolated URI (`:55`), reads one [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto) (`:60`) and maps it into a [`SessionInfo`](#sessioninfo) (`:62-63`). The comment there (`:58-59`) records the contract from the page's side: a missing session answers 404, which arrives as a `NotFound` failure rather than the old `null`, and the pages render exactly the same "session unavailable" state for it.
- **Why it's built this way**: building the dictionary once lets a page label many bookmark or attendance rows with O(1) lookups, while the per-id method serves single-session pages without paying the catalog transfer: exactly the fast and slow split [`ISessionLookupService`](#isessionlookupservice) prescribes. Both reads retry exactly as the token-bearing services in this folder do: `IdempotentReadRetry` hands out the same policy object `AuthenticatedServiceBase` uses (`MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/Api/IdempotentReadRetry.cs:24`), re-sending on an `HttpRequestException`, a 5xx other than 501/505, 408 or 429, up to three times with 2s, 4s, 8s backoff plus jitter (`IdempotentReadRetry.cs:10-12`). Re-sending is safe here only because both calls are GETs, which is why a GET is the helper's sole entry point (`IdempotentReadRetry.cs:35-44`).
- **Where it's used**: registered scoped as `ISessionLookupService` (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:54`). It is also the model the sibling [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice) names in its class doc (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:11-14`) for the search-through, lookup-from-snapshot split. Covered by `SessionLookupServiceTests` (`MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Tests/Services/Lookups/SessionLookupServiceTests.cs`), and named as one of the anonymous lookup callers in the class doc of `IdempotentReadRetryTests` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI.Tests/Services/Api/IdempotentReadRetryTests.cs:12`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the 500-row page size and the 40-page ceiling (20,000 rows) live in `PagedReadAll`, not in this file. If a server kept reporting a larger total than it returns, the loop would stop at the ceiling and `GetAllAsync` would hand back what it had gathered so far without saying so.
